Title: Metal-Dielectric Bilayer Supported Nanoporous Silver Substrates for Ultrasensitive Surface-Enhanced Raman Scattering
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30536
Authors: Maryam Naseer, Anastasiia Sapunova, Ali Douaki, Vincenzo Caligiuri, Silvia Dante, Denis Garoli, Roman Krahne
Abstract: Resolving the chemical footprint of molecules in analytes is of fundamental interest in biosensing and biomedical applications. Surface-enhanced Raman Scattering (SERS) spectroscopy is a powerful tool for non-invasive molecule investigation, and there is a continuous effort to develop highly efficient SERS substrates that can be fabricated at low cost and tuned to the desired spectral range of investigation. Here, we show that the Raman signal of porous silver films can be significantly enhanced by integrating a thin dielectric layer and a metallic backreflector underneath the porous metal film. We call this architecture nanoporous metal on dielectric metal (NPMoDM) and show that it leads to much stronger near-field enhancement in the bulk of the film. Additionally, the spectral band of enhanced sensitivity can be tuned by the thickness of the dielectric layer within the visible spectral range. With rhodamine 6G and para-aminothiophenol as target analytes, we demonstrate SERS detection of substrates incubated with zeptomolar concentrations of the analyte, which corresponds to only few molecules present on a mm2 surface area. Our approach introduces a tunable and scalable (lithography-free) platform for ultrasensitive SERS applications, with a readily accessible surface for analyte functionalization.

### Paper [108]
Title: Correlated chemical and displacive disorder in a lithium-rich disordered rocksalt revealed by electron diffuse scattering
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30537
Authors: Sam Y. Thompson (Center for Sustainable Energy Materials, Department of Chemistry, Aarhus University, Denmark), Kristoffer A. H. St{\o}ckler (Center for Sustainable Energy Materials, Department of Chemistry, Aarhus University, Denmark), Nikolaos Zacharakis (Center for Sustainable Energy Materials, Department of Chemistry, Aarhus University, Denmark), Rebekka Klemmt (Center for Sustainable Energy Materials, Department of Chemistry, Aarhus University, Denmark), Ella M. Schmidt (Faculty of Geosciences, MARUM and MAPEX, University of Bremen, Germany), Sandro Wieser (Institute of Materials Chemistry, TU Wien, Austria), Nico Unglert (Institute of Materials Chemistry, TU Wien, Austria), J{\o}rgen Skibsted (Center for Sustainable Energy Materials, Department of Chemistry, Aarhus University, Denmark), Espen D. B{\o}jesen (Center for Sustainable Energy Materials, Department of Chemistry, Aarhus University, Denmark), Georg K. H. Madsen (Institute of Materials Chemistry, TU Wien, Austria), Bo B. Iversen (Center for Sustainable Energy Materials, Department of Chemistry, Aarhus University, Denmark), Dorthe B. Ravnsb{\ae}k (Center for Sustainable Energy Materials, Department of Chemistry, Aarhus University, Denmark)
Abstract: Lithium transport in disordered rocksalt cathodes depends on local cation arrangements and atomic distortions that are hidden from average-structure analysis. Resolving both contributions experimentally remains challenging, particularly in powders where directional information is lost. Here, we use electron diffuse scattering and three-dimensional difference pair distribution function analysis (3D-{\Delta}PDF) to resolve occupational and displacive correlations in the model lithium-rich rocksalt Li{_2.5}TiO{_3}F{_0.5}. We identify an unusual combination of like-cation first-neighbour and unlike-cation second-neighbour preferences with correlated anion displacements towards Ti and accompanying cation displacements. A minimal pair-interaction model incorporating these displacements reproduces the principal diffuse-scattering and 3D-{\Delta}PDF features and improves agreement with powder-PDF measurements. The experimentally constrained cation configurations exhibit an above-random population of 0-TM lithium-migration environments. These results establish electron 3D-{\Delta}PDF as a route to constrain atomistic models incorporating chemical order and local relaxation, providing a foundation for assessing their combined influence on lithium migration in disordered rocksalt materials.

### Paper [109]
Title: Femtosecond laser-controlled production of functional titanium niobate layers from TiO$_{2}$/Nb$_{2}$O$_{5}$ powders
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30549
Authors: Gonzalo Gomez-Munoz, Aar\'on Calvo-Villoslada, Inmaculada \'Alvarez-Serrano, Mar\'ia Luisa L\'opez, Paloma Fern\'andez, Javier Solis, Bel\'en Sotillo
Abstract: Conventional synthesis methods of titanium niobates (TNO) require prolongated high-temperature treatments. To overcome this limitation, in this work, we have developed a confined powder-bed femtosecond (fs) laser-based technique to sinter TNO films starting from a mixture of TiO$_{2}$ and Nb$_{2}$O$_{5}$ powders, avoiding furnace-based approaches. The influence of laser-processing conditions on film formation, phase composition, and microstructure is systematically investigated, demonstrating that different TNO phases and morphologies can be obtained by controlling the laser pulse energy. A comprehensive characterization of the processed films has been performed, providing insights into the mechanisms governing their formation, which appear to share key features with laser-induced backward transfer (LIBT) processes. Our findings establish the relationship between laser-processing conditions and TNO film formation, highlighting the potential of confined powder-bed fs-laser processing as an alternative route for manufacturing functional oxide layers. Tentative applications of these layers are finally discussed.

### Paper [110]
Title: Asymmetrical transport distribution function: skewness as a key to enhance thermoelectric performance
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30565
Authors: Jin-Cheng Zheng
Abstract: How to achieve high thermoelectric figure of merit is still a scientific challenge. By solving the Boltzmann transport equation, thermoelectric properties can be written as integrals of a single function, the transport distribution function (TDF). In this work, the shape effects of transport distribution function in various typical functional forms on thermoelectric properties of materials are systematically investigated. It is found that the asymmetry of TDF, characterized by skewness, can be used to describe universally the trend of thermoelectric properties. By defining symmetric and asymmetric TDF functions, a novel skewness is then constructed for thermoelectric applications. It is demonstrated, by comparison with ab initio calculations and experiments, that the proposed thermoelectric skewness not only perfectly captures the main feature of conventional skewness, but also is able to predict the thermoelectric power accurately. This comparison confirms the unique feature of our proposed thermoelectric skewness, as well as its special role of connection between the statistics of TDF and thermoelectric properties of materials. It is also found that the thermoelectric performance can be enhanced by increasing the asymmetry of TDF. Finally, it is also interesting to find that the thermoelectric transport properties based on typical quantum statistics (Fermi-Dirac distributions), can be well described by typical shape parameter (skewness) for classical statistics.

### Paper [111]
Title: Electric Field Assisted Charge Transfer Mediated Electronic Coupling in Ferromagnetic/Multiferroic Hetero-structure
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30598
Authors: Subho Saha, Sourav Chowdhury, Suryakanta Mondal, Kartick Biswas, Deepak Prajapat, Andrei Gloskovskii, Suresh Bharadwaj, Mukul Gupta, Pavan Nukala, Bhagwati Prasad, Ram Janay Choudhary
Abstract: The strongly coupled ferromagnet-multiferroic material could be a better alternative to complementary metal-oxide semiconductor (CMOS) based data storage device or rather lone multiferroic oxide materials because it offers the electric field controlled conductivity and large magnetisation at the interfaces, but choosing a proper system is still very much challenging. In the present scenario, an interionic charge transfer from Co ion to Fe ion via oxygen is observed at the interfacial region of LaCoO3 (LCO)/BiFeO3 (BFO) grown on SrTiO3 substrate, leading to induce multivalency in Co, Fe ion and also induces exchange biasing in the system. The charge transferring between Co and Fe ions gets further enhanced by electric biasing across the bilayer in the low temperature region, which affects the polarisation behaviour of BFO layer. This confirms the electronic coupling between the ferromagnetic LCO and the multiferroic BFO layers, useful for the making of voltage-controlled magnetic anisotropy (VCMA) systems.

### Paper [112]
Title: Coupled Altermagnetic and Valley Physics in Monolayer VBiSe$_3$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30649
Authors: Divyanshi Tyagi, Saswata Bhattacharya
Abstract: The interplay of compensated magnetism, spin--orbit coupling (SOC), and valley degrees of freedom provides a promising route toward spin--valley control without conventional ferromagnetism. Here, using first-principles calculations combined with Wannier-based analysis, we reveal coupled altermagnetic and valley physics in monolayer VBiSe$_3$. In the absence of SOC, the compensated N\'eel antiferromagnetic state, whose opposite-spin V sublattices are related only by mirror operations, exhibits momentum-dependent spin splitting with a characteristic $i$-wave altermagnetic texture. Upon inclusion of SOC, the conduction-band states develop anisotropic Rashba-like spin splitting together with a sizable $K/K'$ valley splitting of about $112.2$~meV. Orbital analysis and an effective SOC Hamiltonian identify the hybridization between SOC-active Se-$p_{x,y}$ states and predominantly V-$d_{z^2}$ conduction-band states as an important microscopic origin of the valley splitting. The two valleys carry Berry curvature of the same sign but different magnitude, unlike the opposite-sign pattern of conventional valley Hall systems, so the large valley splitting, rather than a valley Hall effect, offers a possible route to valley-selective carrier occupation by gating. Moreover, a modest $-1\%$ biaxial compressive strain drives the system into a quasi-half-valley-metallic state with valley-polarized electrons and non-valley-polarized holes. These results establish monolayer VBiSe$_3$ as a promising platform for coupled altermagnetic, spin--orbit, and valley phenomena with strain-tunable electronic states.

### Paper [113]
Title: Strain-induced non-trivial band topology and large magnetoresistance in epitaxial SrTaO$_3$ thin films
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30681
Authors: Aditya Mehta, Soumyakanta Panda, Pratik Sahu, Amit Chauhan, Dibyajyoti Sahoo, Shwetha G Bhat, Rohit Soni, Goutam Tripathy, Niharika Mohapatra, B. R. K. Nanda, D. Samal
Abstract: Epitaxial thin films of complex transition metal oxides constitute an ideal platform for realizing correlated topological phases through strain engineering of crystal symmetry and band topology. Here, we investigate the electronic transport properties and the band structure of the 5$d^1$ paramagnetic metal SrTaO$_3$ thin film under epitaxial compressive strain. The film exhibits a large non-saturating quasi-quadratic magnetoresistance (MR) and a nonlinear Hall effect characteristic of two-band transport, together with the field induced re-entrant metallic behavior. The violation of Kohler's scaling further corroborates the scattering from multiple bands. Semiclassical two-band analysis yields carrier densities and mobilities of the order $\sim 10^{21}~\mathrm{cm^{-3}}$ and $\sim 10^{4}~\mathrm{cm^2 V^{-1} s^{-1}}$, respectively. The observed large mobility and unsaturated MR, indicates the presence of a non-trivial band structure. Consistent with this, results from density functional theory calculations and tight binding model studies reveal that the stable structure has an octahedral rotation $a^0a^0c^+$, where the non-symmorphic symmetry ensures the presence of topological nodal lines yielding finite Berry curvature in the vicinity of the Fermi level. Besides the nodal line, trivial electron and hole pockets exist near the Fermi level, contributing to electron transport. These findings establish that epitaxial strain drives SrTaO$_3$ into a nontrivial topological semimetal, a phase inaccessible in the bulk counterpart.

### Paper [114]
Title: The effect of isotopically-enriched 70Ge on the Raman scattering in Cr2Ge2Te6
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30688
Authors: Grzegorz Krasucki, Katarzyna Olkowska-Pucko, Tomasz Wo\'zniak, Mihai I. Sturza, Holger Kohlmann, Adam Babi\'nski, Maciej R. Molas
Abstract: The highest-energy A5g Raman-active vibrational mode in chromium germanium telluride (Cr2Ge2Te6, CGT) exhibits a unique lineshape comprising a series of remarkably narrow peaks. While this multiplet was attributed to the natural isotope distribution of germanium affecting Ge-Ge pseudo-molecular vibrations, direct evidence has been lacking. Here, we present a Raman spectroscopy study of CGT isotopically enriched with 70Ge to validate this hypothesis. Upon isotopic enrichment, both the E5g and A5g modes exhibit a pronounced blue-shift. At room temperature, the A5g peak undergoes significant narrowing, while low-temperature measurements reveal a single, well-defined line instead of the previously observed multiplet. These observations align precisely with density functional theory calculations, confirming that the multi-line profile originates from Ge isotope distribution. Our findings demonstrate that similar isotope-induced Raman splitting may occur in other structurally analogous layered tellurides containing Ge or Si.

### Paper [115]
Title: All-in-one Spintronic Sensory Neurons for Magnetic Edge Intelligence
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30740
Authors: Jiajia Jian, Bin Rong, Jiaxin Liu, Fangyi Zhao, Kai Li, Tao Huang, Xue Zhou, Xin Yue, Zeyu Ma, Xuefeng Zheng, Weidong Zhang, Jian Fu Zhang, Yumeng Yang, Zheng Chai
Abstract: Magnetic fields enable non-contact sensing through nonmagnetic materials, offering opportunities for magnetic edge intelligence in autonomous recognition, navigation, and control. However, directly converting magnetic stimuli into neural spikes for local neuromorphic processing remains challenging. Here, we demonstrate an all-in-one spintronic sensory neuron (SSN) based on spin-transfer-torque magnetic tunnel junctions (STT-MTJs) fabricated using a 40-nm magnetoresistive random-access memory (MRAM) process. By exploiting magnetic-field-modulated stochastic switching, the SSN enables probability-based sensing with an electrically tunable sensitive field window, allowing adaptive operation across different magnetic-field ranges without structural modification. This tunability alleviates the fixed sensitivity-range constraint of conventional tunneling magnetoresistance sensing, providing an accessible sensing range of +/-40 mT with an average probability sensitivity of 2.91%/mT. Importantly, the same stochastic switching process directly transduces magnetic stimuli into binary spike trains, integrating magnetic sensing, nonlinear rate encoding, and spike generation within a single nanoscale device. The resulting sigmoidal spike-rate response enables magnetic-field encoding, experimentally validated using the Neural Engineering Framework. Using experimentally characterized device responses, a simulated 10 x 10 SSN array achieves 90.7% accuracy in contactless magnetic-trajectory recognition with optimized encoding, approaching the software baseline. These results establish a scalable, MRAM-compatible approach to unifying programmable magnetic sensing and neural encoding for sensor-native edge intelligence.

### Paper [116]
Title: Two-dimensional Thyristor
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30748
Authors: Purbasha Ray, Rupali Verma, Biswajeet Nayak, Suman Kumar Chakraborty, Mayank Shrivastava, Prasana Kumar Sahoo
Abstract: Two-dimensional (2D) semiconductors offer a viable platform for next-generation electronics beyond sub-3 nm nodes, yet essential nonlinear circuit elements, such as thyristors, remain unexplored on atomically thin platforms. Here, we report an atomically thin reconfigurable electro-optical thyristor based on a lateral WSe2-MoSe2-WSe2-MoSe2 PNPN heterostructure, directly grown via sequential edge epitaxy. Unlike single-junction 2D diodes, this multijunction architecture exhibits thyristor-like nonlinear switching, including forward blocking, abrupt snapback, bistability, and electrical latching. The device exhibits a forward-blocking voltage of 183 V, corresponding to a critical electric field of 1 MV/cm. Under open-gate conditions, avalanche-assisted carrier multiplication and regenerative feedback are associated with a sharp forward-voltage drop of 175 V, a pronounced current surge, and an ultralow holding voltage. Gate-pulse modulation and temperature-dependent measurements indicate avalanche-assisted thyristor operation and latching behavior. This 2D lateral heterostructure enables a new route to nonlinear and bistable switching, latching, ultralow standby power, and adaptive logic, opening the way for reconfigurable 2D electronics and photonic integrated circuits.

### Paper [117]
Title: Aharonov-Bohm oscillations in transport through interacting quantum dots
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30753
Authors: Oliver Oing, Alexander Hahn, J\"urgen K\"onig, Alfred Hucht
Abstract: We investigate electronic transport through a two-terminal Aharonov-Bohm interferometer with two quantum dots in the presence of finite intra-dot Coulomb interactions. With the help of a diagrammatic technique, we derive, within linear perturbation theory in the tunnelling-coupling strength, analytic expressions for the occupations, current, and interferometric visibility in terms of a coherence measure that involves the sum of four off-diagonal elements of the reduced density matrix. The latter are calculated numerically from a master equation for arbitrary settings of the system parameters. For some symmetric setups, we obtain closed-form expressions for the linear conductance. We systematically analyse the impact of a finite Coulomb interaction on the interferometric visibility, and reveal a rapid suppression of destructive interference upon breaking of system symmetries. Our results are supported by alternative methods, including the TraSPI method.

### Paper [118]
Title: Geometry-controlled clogging and transport mechanisms in confined Y-junctions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30758
Authors: Horacio Javier Diaz Carrera, Diego J. P\'erez-Morelo, Sergio J. Manzi, Paulo M. Centres
Abstract: We investigate transport and clogging of hard particles in a confined symmetric Y-junction using off-lattice Monte Carlo simulations. The geometry is controlled by the channel width w and a truncation parameter s that modifies the inner vertex. The flow probability exhibits a sharp sigmoidal crossover, defining a characteristic width wc that depends non-monotonically on s. This non-monotonic behavior persists under variations of the transverse Monte Carlo step, although the absolute values of wc change. Microscopic analysis reveals two distinct blocking mechanisms: single-particle obstruction at small truncations and collective arch-like structures at larger truncations. Residence-time statistics and particle-order inversions provide additional evidence of this change in transport dynamics. Our results demonstrate that local modifications of junction geometry can strongly affect clogging and particle transport, even in a minimal model with excluded-volume interactions only.

### Paper [119]
Title: Janus Asymmetry Unlocks Rashba Spintronics and Chiral Phononics in Two-Dimensional Carbo-Chalcogenides
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30767
Authors: Valentina Barreca, Giorgio Sangiovanni, Giovanni Cantele, Ludovica Zullo
Abstract: Spintronics and chiral phononics offer complementary routes for nanoscale control, yet realizing both functionalities within a common two-dimensional platform remains challenging. Here, using extensive first-principles calculations, we show that Janus asymmetry simultaneously drives both effects in transition-metal carbo-chalcogenides (JTMCCs) along two chemically distinct routes, through either chalcogen or transition-metal substitution. Remarkably, both Janus routes yield giant Rashba responses, with $\alpha_\mathrm{R}\approx2$ eV {\AA} in Ta$_2$CSSe and NbTaCS$_2$, exceeding those reported for Mo/W-based Janus transition-metal dichalcogenides. Furthermore, Janus asymmetry lifts the degeneracy of opposite-chirality phonons, producing splittings as large as $\approx28$ cm$^{-1}$. More strikingly, asymmetry generates chiral carbon vibrations that were achiral in the centrosymmetric parent compounds, with splittings reaching $\approx8$ cm$^{-1}$. Finally, we show external electric fields tunability over Rashba strength and chiral-phonon splittings, providing a common control knob for electronic spin and lattice angular momentum. Our work offers a unified strategy for realizing spintronic and chiral phononic in two-dimensional materials.

### Paper [120]
Title: Perfect Spin-to-Charge Signal Conversion via Inverse Spin Hall Effect in a Superconductor
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30781
Authors: Rodrigo de las Heras, Ilya V. Tokatly, F. Sebastian Bergeret
Abstract: Spin-to-charge conversion is central to spintronics, but conventional spin Hall mechanisms are intrinsically limited by the small spin Hall angle. Here we show that superconductivity can overcome this limitation and enable near-unity spin-to-charge signal conversion. We consider a nonlocal spin-valve geometry in which a spin accumulation is injected into a superconductor and converted into a transverse charge signal through the inverse spin Hall effect. In the superconducting state, the strongly suppressed quasiparticle conductance below the gap allows the resulting charge imbalance to generate a voltage comparable to the injected spin bias, despite a small spin Hall angle. We derive the full nonequilibrium response including finite spectral broadening and finite temperature, and propose temperature and spin-bias-dependent measurements of the detector voltage to test our predictions.

### Paper [121]
Title: Multipartite entanglement structure of crystal quenches
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30795
Authors: Paul Gaudin, Cl\'ement Berthi\`ere
Abstract: Understanding how entanglement emerges and spreads in out-of-equilibrium quantum many-body systems is a fundamental problem. In this paper, we investigate multipartite entanglement after quantum quenches. In particular, we study the entanglement structure and dynamics of quasiparticle multiplets generated by crystal quenches in the tight-binding chain. We identify zero-configurations for which a multiplet shared between three parties has no irreducible tripartite entanglement---these configurations consist entirely of pairwise distributed bipartite-entangled states. The existence of zero-configurations has important implications on the entanglement structure and dynamics of crystal quenches. For example, we show that it can further delay the production of tripartite entanglement. We build the quasiparticle picture for the Markov gap, and prove that it is a faithful witness of irreducible tripartite entanglement for crystal quenches. We discuss several key aspects of their tripartite entanglement dynamics through the lens of the Markov gap. We confront our results with exact lattice numerics and find perfect agreement in the hydrodynamic regime.

### Paper [122]
Title: Gallium at germanium surfaces: links to superconducting films and correlated adatom lattices
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30809
Authors: Peter Jacobson
Abstract: Epitaxial films grown on Ge(001) show that germanium hyperdoped with substitutional Ga is superconducting. In earlier implanted films, Ga-rich precipitates obscured the origin of the superconductivity. This review examines the sites Ga occupies on Ge(001) and Ge(111) and how it enters the growing film. The Ga fraction expected from the growth fluxes is 17.9\%, yet the distribution of Ga among lattice sites is unknown. Comparisons include Ga on Si(001) and Si(111),In, Sn, Pb, Sb and P on Ge(001), and elemental and two-dimensional Ga. Where its surface coordination has been established, Ga has three bonds, whereas substitutional Ga in the films is fourfold coordinated and acts as an acceptor. Scanning tunnelling microscopy and X-ray standing waves have assigned Ga sites on Ge(111), but the dilute Ga site on Ge(001), where the films are grown, has yet to be measured. On Ge(111), Ga in the substrate may supply holes to the correlated Sn adatom lattice, as B supplies holes to the superconducting Sn lattice on Si(111). Whether Ga can supply holes depends on where it sits and whether it has three or four neighbours. On both surfaces, element-specific measurements and calculations comparing adsorption with substitution could settle the remaining site assignments.

### Paper [123]
Title: Altermagnetism of MnSiN2 and MnGeN2 in a wurtzite-based crystal structure
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30865
Authors: Ilteris K. Turan, Jonathan Agrinya, Walter R. L. Lambrecht
Abstract: Based on symmetry analysis, the Pna2_1 wurtzite-based crystal structure with Mn as a magnetic group-II element, MnSiN2 and MnGeN2 belong to the class of altermagnets because spin-up and spin-down Mn in the unit cell are related by rotation or mirror plane type operations rather than inversion or translation. Spin polarized density functional theory calculations and quasiparticle self-consistent (QS) GW calculations support the symmetry analysis and identify the lowest-energy spin configuration as Pna'2_1' in agreement with neutron diffraction data. The altermagnetic band splittings allowed for the three different antiferromagnetic ordering patterns are reported and symmetry analyzed. Additionally, spin wave dispersions, chiral magnon spltting, and Neel temperature estimates from the mean-field and random-phase approximations are provided, as well as calculations of the optical dielectric function using the Bethe-Salpeter Equation method. MnSiN2 exhibits both Wannier-type excitons with N-p to Si-s transition character and charge-transfer Frenkel-type excitons with hole on N-p and electron on Mn-d orbital character.

### Paper [124]
Title: Interaction-driven ordering of multiple pseudospins and anomalous Hall metals in Bernal-stacked graphene
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30906
Authors: Jeonghyeon Suh, Kyungjin Shin, Hongki Min
Abstract: Recent observations of anomalous Hall states in Bernal-stacked tetralayer graphene raise a fundamental question: How do electron-electron interactions organize multiple low-energy pseudospin sectors within each spin-valley flavor? Within the Hartree-Fock approximation, we analyze interaction-driven pseudospin ordering and spin-valley polarization as functions of displacement field and carrier density. We find a rich phase diagram shaped by the interplay between the two pseudospin doublets, including mixed flavor orders at charge neutrality and anomalous Hall metals upon doping. In particular, we identify topological states with $\sigma_{xy} \approx 6e^{2}/h$, consistent with recent experimental observations. Our results highlight the central role of multiple pseudospin doublets in the interaction-driven phases of even-layer Bernal multilayers.

### Paper [125]
Title: Statistical mechanics of noisy collisions in active matter: Dyson-Landau theory
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30910
Authors: Horst-Holger Boltz, Max-Robin Schultz, Thomas Ihle
Abstract: Active systems and other coarse-grained descriptions are often modeled by generalized dynamics, effective equations of motion that are not bound by the constraints of genuinely microscopic physics. In particular, these equations are generally stochastic on the smallest resolved scale. This poses a challenge to first-principles statistical physics, which usually derives emergent noise on larger scales from deterministic interactions. Here, we show how to systematically incorporate microscopic noise into kinetic theory in the limit of weak noise, i.e., high activity. By means of a Dyson expansion of the noisy free propagator, we derive the leading correction to the Landau kinetic equation for weakly interacting systems. We find that the common practice of combining a deterministic collision integral with the diffusive terms of the Fokker--Planck equation is the correct leading order. For dry aligning active matter, however, the corrections are non-analytic in the noise strength, as nearly parallel particles interact for arbitrarily long times. For noise that acts only during interactions, a minimal model of stochastic interactions, noise and collisions do not decouple: the leading corrections are odd in the coupling strength, partly logarithmically enhanced, and amount to an orientation-dependent effective noise strength. Agent-based simulations confirm the predicted dependence on the sign of the coupling. Even in this minimal model, the effect of noise on collisions can thus not be inferred from symmetry alone.

### Paper [126]
Title: Molecular Pawns on a Surface Chessboard: Computational Investigation of Nanographenes with Atomistic Methods
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30917
Authors: Antonio Lorenzo Mariano, Jacopo Lupi, Emilio Lorini, Matteo Briganti
Abstract: Atomically precise nanographenes combine tunable electronic topology with localized, coherent spin states, making them candidate platforms for molecular quantum technologies. This review examines the multiscale theoretical framework governing these systems: the hierarchy of computational methods for describing magnetic properties, the substrate-molecule interface, on-surface synthetic kinetics, and supramolecular self-assembly. We highlight how density functional theory, automated reaction-path discovery, and spin-aware machine-learned interatomic potentials connect static electronic structure to mesoscale dynamics, and how magnetic exchange, optical excitations, and spin transport can be predicted before synthesis.

### Paper [127]
Title: Dirac-like bands and weak-field transverse transport in ABA trilayer graphene
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30924
Authors: Agustin Montemurro, Fujia Lu, Ellis R. Huntley, Vladimir Fal'ko
Abstract: The low-energy electronic structure of ABA trilayer graphene is composed of light and heavy electron branches, which can be altered by an out-of-plane displacement field. Here, we show that, depending on the values of Slonczewski-Weiss-McClure parameters, a displacement field can inflict a band edge inversion, for which we identify the inversion conditions. This inversion is accompanied by a reversal of Berry curvature and giant topological magnetic moments at the band edges. In a weak perpendicular magnetic field, those topological magnetic moments produce valley splitting, giving rise to distinct anomalous transverse electrical and thermoelectric responses. These features offer a potential route to detecting the band inversion and constraining several of the least established tight-binding parameters, such as the on-site energy shift for the dimer pair of carbons $\Delta^\prime$ and the next neighbour hopping parameters $\gamma_2$ and $\gamma_5$.

### Paper [128]
Title: Generalized dielectric-dependent hybrid functional combining Gaussian and Yukawa screening
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30930
Authors: Alexey Tal, Fabien Tran, Wei Chen, Stefan Riemelmoser, Alfredo Pasquarello
Abstract: We develop a generalized Coulomb-attenuated method (GCAM) for dielectric-dependent hybrid functionals that combines Gaussian and Yukawa-type screening functions to provide an accurate representation of the random-phase approximation (RPA) dielectric function. Through systematic fitting to RPA dielectric functions of semiconductors and insulators, and to the Lindhard dielectric function of the homogeneous electron gas, we demonstrate that GCAM significantly reduces errors in the model dielectric function across the full momentum range. However, despite this improved physical description of screening, band gap calculations reveal that GCAM yields larger errors compared to experiment than previous CAM approaches for wide-gap materials, but smaller errors for most narrow-gap materials. This counterintuitive result demonstrates that the success of CAM stems not purely from physical reasons, but also from a partial cancellation of errors. In contrast, for optical absorption spectra calculated within a model Bethe-Salpeter equation framework, GCAM systematically outperforms CAM, showing better agreement with the reference spectra obtained with the diagonal RPA screening in both peak positions and relative intensities. Our findings reveal the important role of error cancellation in dielectric-dependent hybrid functionals.

### Paper [129]
Title: Donnan Grand Canonical Monte Carlo Simulations with Fast Ewald Summation for Calculating Individual Ionic Chemical Potentials
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30961
Authors: Alexandre P. dos Santos, Amin Bakhshandeh, Yan Levin
Abstract: We present a Donnan Grand Canonical Monte Carlo (GCMC) simulation method using fast Ewald summation to accurately and efficiently calculate the individual ionic excess chemical potentials of $\alpha_+:\alpha_-$ electrolyte solutions. By introducing a dynamic inner electrostatic potential that acts to penalize net charge fluctuations and maintain average charge neutrality ($\langle Q_t \rangle = 0$), the method enables single-ion GCMC trial moves without sacrificing convergence speed or breaking thermodynamic consistency. Unlike the Widom particle insertion method---which suffers from severe phase-space sampling bottlenecks and requires separate virtual insertions for each ionic species---the Donnan GCMC protocol simultaneously yields the individual excess chemical potentials $\mu_+^{\text{ex}}$ and $\mu_-^{\text{ex}}$ corresponding to a resulting bulk concentration $c_b$ with rapid numerical convergence. Varying the reference activity parameter $c_r$ allows us to efficiently construct the complete $\mu_\pm^{\text{ex}}(c_b)$ curves. Comparison with the Mean Spherical Approximation (MSA) demonstrates excellent agreement for size-symmetric electrolytes, while highlighting the failure of linear-response theories for size-asymmetric ions where electrostatic correlations and cavity effects become strongly coupled.

### Paper [130]
Title: Magneto-ARPES as a direct probe of Berry Curvature and the Orbital Magnetic Moment
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30968
Authors: Seohyun Kong, Bernd Braunecker, Luke C. Rhodes
Abstract: The Berry curvature and orbital magnetic moment are central to the understanding of electronic transport phenomena such as the anomalous Hall effect, yet measuring these quantities directly with momentum resolution remains experimentally challenging. Here we show that angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy measurements in a weak magnetic field (magneto-ARPES) provide a direct route to both quantities. We develop an analytic theory of photoemission in a weak magnetic field and show that, for any material, the first-order change in photoemission intensity is directly proportional to both the Berry curvature and the orbital magnetic moment, even in the presence of self-energy effects. For linearly polarised light, the matrix elements are unchanged to first order in the field, so the intensity change directly reflects these quantities. Using monolayer WSe$_2$ as an example, we show how both quantities can be extracted in a typical experiment. This finding thus points to magneto-ARPES as a general route for measuring the momentum-resolved Berry curvature and orbital magnetic moment in quantum materials.

### Paper [131]
Title: Interface-Engineered Piezoelectricity and Electromechanical Coupling in Two-Dimensional van der Waals Heterostructures
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30974
Authors: Shivanand Yadav, Raihan Ahammed, Umer Ali, B. S. Bhadoria, Yogesh S. Chauhan, Amit Agarwal, Somnath Bhowmick
Abstract: Interfaces between dissimilar two-dimensional (2D) materials provide a route to engineer electromechanical responses that are not intrinsic to the individual constituent layers. Here, we use high-throughput density functional theory (DFT) calculations to investigate 461 van der Waals heterostructures constructed from 57 2D monolayers and establish a materials-design framework linking work-function mismatch, interfacial charge redistribution, dipole formation, and strain-dependent electromechanical response. We find that heterostructure formation generates out-of-plane interfacial dipoles via charge redistribution between the constituent layers, while biaxial in-plane strain systematically modulates them. The resulting heterostructures exhibit two distinct regimes: metallic interfaces with strain-tunable interfacial dipoles and semiconducting heterostructures with well-defined piezoelectric responses. For the latter, Berry-phase (BP) calculations independently validate the charge-density-difference (CDD) approach, which is generally applicable to all representative systems, with piezoelectric coefficients calculated via two methods agreeing to within 10%. Among 24 selected heterostructures, effective biaxial interfacial electromechanical coefficients reach 83.35 pCm-1 . These results demonstrate that the interface can serve as the active element governing electromechanical coupling and provide a computationally guided strategy for designing strain-tunable out-of-plane electromechanical responses in 2D van der Waals heterostructures.

### Paper [132]
Title: Terahertz detection of magnon chirality in polycrystalline CsCuCl$_3$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30992
Authors: Juan S. Rodr\'iguez-Hern\'andez, Gabriel F. Freitas, Nicolas M. Kawahala, Rafael S. Freitas, Felix G. G. Hernandez
Abstract: In antiferromagnets, the handedness of magnon precession is usually measured on single crystals. In a pressed pellet of the chiral helimagnet CsCuCl$_3$, whose grains carry both structural handednesses and all orientations, we found that, from 5 to 7 T, the sub-terahertz magnon absorbs one circular polarization more than 20 times as strongly as the other, which rules out precession about each grain's own axis. Spin-wave theory attributes this to the Zeeman frequency, here two to four times the exchange-enhanced gap, which locks the precession axis to the field in every grain. Magnon chirality in antiferromagnets is thus accessible without a single crystal.

### Paper [133]
Title: Self-consistent Floquet theory of steady-state ordering in driven open systems
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30996
Authors: Srinjoy Ghosh (IUB), Babak Seradjeh (IUB), Arijit Kundu
Abstract: Periodically driven open systems have quasienergy bands whose steady-state occupations are set by the drive and reservoir. We formulate a self-consistent Floquet spectral theory of electronic ordering in the weak-reservoir-coupling limit, expressing the nonlinear mean-field equation and the particle-hole response in terms of quasienergies, steady-state occupations, and harmonic matrix elements that account for micromotion. We illustrate the theory in the square-lattice Hubbard model and show how the drive produces `dynamical nesting' conditions and how Floquet resonances produce \textit{hysteretic} responses due to multiple self-consistent solutions. Interestingly, we find that the hysteresis branches exhibit distinct Floquet topologies. Away from half-filling, we find that the combination of drive-induced detuning of the van Hove saddle points and the micromotion leads to the onset of switching between competing incommensurate orders.

### Paper [134]
Title: Fast-erasure energy-cost lower bound and its first-order correction under restricted control
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31007
Authors: Yihang Cai (Department of Physics and Shenzhen Research Institute, Xiamen University, Xiamen, China), Yirong Jiang (Department of Physics and Shenzhen Research Institute, Xiamen University, Xiamen, China), Chenlong Huang (Department of Physics and Shenzhen Research Institute, Xiamen University, Xiamen, China), Yue Liu (Center for Gravitational Physics and Quantum Information, Yukawa Institute for Theoretical Physics, Kyoto University, Kyoto, Japan), Dahai He (Department of Physics and Shenzhen Research Institute, Xiamen University, Xiamen, China)
Abstract: Determining the minimum work required for information erasure is central to understanding the energetic limits of fast and reliable information processing. In the fast-erasure limit, we derive an explicit expression for this minimum work in a one-dimensional overdamped isothermal system with control restricted to linear and quadratic additions to a reference potential. For symmetric initial distributions at fixed mobility, the minimum work scales inversely with the erasure duration, with a prefactor determined solely by the second moment and a quantile associated with the target success probability. We further derive the leading finite-time correction at a prescribed success probability and verify both analytical predictions numerically. These results provide an analytical benchmark for fast erasure under restricted control, connecting a universal leading cost with finite-time corrections that account for the detailed form of the memory potential.

### Paper [135]
Title: LeIFF: A localized-electron and ion force field for coupled nuclear and electronic dynamics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31029
Authors: Anthony Mannino, Isidro Losada, Simon Divilov, Michelle Fritz, Younos Hashem, Ashraf Mrabet, Paula Mori S\'anchez, Javier Junquera, Mariv\'i Fern\'andez-Serra, Jose M. Soler
Abstract: Classical and machine-learned interatomic potentials enable large-scale simulations by replacing electronic structure with an effective energy surface for nuclear motion. This excludes electronic dynamics, electronic excitation and energy exchange between electrons and ions. First-principles electron dynamics can describe these processes, but its computational cost restricts system sizes and simulation times. Electron force fields offer an intermediate description by treating electrons as semiclassical dynamical particles interacting with ions. We introduce the localized-electron and ion force field (LeIFF), a compact physical model for both ground state bonding and excited electron dynamics. Valence electrons are represented by spin-labeled spherical charge distributions with dynamical centers and widths. The energy combines analytic electronic kinetic, Coulomb, and dispersion terms with spline-interpolated short-range interactions fitted to a large molecular data set. The resulting energy surface describes a variety of ground state properties across diverse bonding environments and charge states. It also provides a qualitative description of electronic behavior away from equilibrium. It shows improved robustness, accuracy, and transferability relative to earlier electron force-fields. Without any solid-state training, the same model describes dielectric screening and collective electronic response in silicon. LeIFF thus opens a route to large-scale simulations of nonadiabatic processes in molecules and solids, combining chemical bonding and nonequilibrium electronic motion within a common physical framework.

### Paper [136]
Title: Quantum and classical transport in the underscreened Kondo lattice
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31093
Authors: Leo Pintar, Don Rolih, Rok \v{Z}itko
Abstract: We investigate quantum effects in high-spin Kondo-lattice transport as resistivity approaches the classical spin-disorder limit. Using dynamical mean-field theory and the numerical renormalization group, we calculate resistivity, the Seebeck coefficient, and optical conductivity for the paramagnetic single-channel Kondo lattice away from half filling. At intermediate temperatures, the resistivity for large local spin $S$ approaches the double-exchange plateau. Thermopower retains a positive quantum excess even where resistivity is nearly classical. The excess arises from the transport kernel's particle-hole asymmetry. Even the full Kelvin approximation, often accurate for incoherent correlated metals, captures only part of it. Incomplete Kondo screening leaves residual moments and leads to singular Fermi-liquid behavior at low temperatures. For $S=1$, the apparent power-law resistivity is consistent with a $1/\ln^2(T_\rho/T)$ asymptote. Above the Kondo scale $T_K$, weak-coupling resistivity follows $1/\ln^2(T/T_K)$. Above the band and exchange scales, the charge susceptibility decreases as $1/T$, while the effective diffusion constant becomes nearly temperature independent, resulting in linear resistivity. A many-body coherent-potential approximation accounts for the spin dependence of the linear coefficient without adjustable parameters.

### Paper [137]
Title: Lincege.jl: LINked Cluster Expansions on a GEneral GEometry
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31102
Authors: Pranav Seetharaman, Francisco Correia, Ehsan Khatami
Abstract: The numerical linked-cluster expansion (NLCE) is a powerful tool for studying properties of quantum lattice models directly in the thermodynamic limit. Here, we introduce Lincege.jl, a versatile, efficient, and easy-to-use package for generating the clusters needed to perform the NLCE on a general finite or infinite lattice. Lincege.jl takes as input only the Bravais lattice vectors, the basis information, and the expansion unit and uses a depth-first search to efficiently generate clusters, their lattice constants, and the sub-cluster information. It outputs final coefficients of the series, expressed in terms of the extensive properties of the clusters, in different orders. The package also contains tools for visualizing the lattice and the generated clusters as well as a classical Ising model solver that can be used as a template for interfacing Lincege.jl with solvers of other lattice models. This package can be installed using the Julia package manager and utilized with example scripts from our GitHub repository at https://github.com/rightleftspin/Lincege.jl. The online documentation, including examples, can be found at https://rightleftspin.github.io/Lincege.jl/stable/.

### Paper [138]
Title: Self-similar Inequality Dynamics in Biased Competition for a Conserved Resource
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31103
Authors: Giacomo Bartolucci, Jonathan Bauermann
Abstract: In many complex systems the redistribution of a conserved resource can generate strong inequality. Important examples are cities exchanging inhabitants, farms competing for farmland, or agents for wealth in saturated markets. Here we model such systems as agents competing for a fixed total resource, and show that coupling agent extinction to a bias favoring richer agents dynamically amplifies this inequality over time. Drawing on an analogy with emulsions, where droplets compete for a conserved quantity, we introduce a dynamical critical resource size that determines an agent's fate. Crucially, this threshold grows in time, continuously raising the bar for survival and further reinforcing inequality. Increasing the bias strength, we find a transition from a continuous resource concentration towards a winner-takes-all outcome. Furthermore, the case of continuous resource concentration is characterized by a self-similar dynamics accompanied by a power-law or exponential increase in inequality, quantified by the Gini coefficient. We derive these results analytically in a mean-field model and compare them to agent-based simulations, showing excellent agreement.

### Paper [139]
Title: Anomalous Field-induced Coherence beyond Single-ion Kondo Physics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31137
Authors: Arushi, Konstantin Semeniuk, Manuel Brando, Javier F. Landaeta, Elena Hassinger, Christoph Geibel
Abstract: The emergence of coherent heavy quasiparticles in Kondo lattices is generally accompanied by a reduction in electrical resistivity at low temperatures. However, certain strongly correlated metals exhibit anomalously large resistivities that persist to very low temperatures, raising fundamental questions about the mechanisms governing charge transport. Here, we report an unusual magnetic field-induced evolution of electronic transport and thermodynamic properties in a Ce-based heavy fermion compound, Ce$_{2}$Rh$_{3}$Ga$_{5}$. At zero field, Ce$_{2}$Rh$_{3}$Ga$_{5}$ undergoes a magnetic transition at $T$$_{0}$ = 0.45 K and exhibits a strongly enhanced low temperature specific heat coefficient, reaching $C$/$T$ = 2.2 J/mol$_{Ce}$-K$^{2}$, but no discernible coherence in its electrical resistivity. Remarkably, applying a magnetic field produces a large negative magnetoresistance and restores coherent metallic behavior, characterized by low-temperature $T$$^2$ resistivity. Although the field dependence of the electronic specific heat coefficient can be described phenomenologically within a single-ion Kondo framework, the same description fails to account for the strong suppression of the residual resistivity and the overall magnetoresistance. These results point to an additional field-sensitive scattering contribution beyond conventional single-ion Kondo physics. The striking resemblance to the long-standing anomalous magnetotransport behavior of UBe$_{13}$, without the complications introduced by superconductivity, establishes Ce$_{2}$Rh$_{3}$Ga$_{5}$ as a platform for investigating the microscopic origin of anomalous scattering and field-induced coherent transport in strongly correlated electron systems.

### Paper [140]
Title: Protected Charge Readout Scheme Utilizing Nonlinear Resonators
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31144
Authors: Andrea Cicovic, Harald Havir, Pierre Glidic, Samuel Andersson, Sebastian Lehmann, Kimberly A. Dick, Claes Thelander, Ville F. Maisi
Abstract: Charge detection of nanoscale quantum devices commonly relies on dissipative sensors, where signal absorption causes measurement backaction. Recently, nonlinear resonators showed a near-unity readout signal through an induced frequency shift. We show that this frequency shift protects the sensor, as it detunes the resonator from the measurement signal. The resonator excitation reduces while an enhanced readout signal is maintained. Our results, supported by numerical modeling, show that nonlinear resonators can combine strong readout contrast with reduced measurement-induced backaction.

### Paper [141]
Title: Solute-Aware Stochastic Korhonen Model of Electromigration in Wide vs Near-Bamboo Al-Cu Lines
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31206
Authors: Narahara Chari Dingari, David R. Heskett
Abstract: We couple an evolving Cu field to the one-dimensional Korhonen stress equation on random grain maps for wide (10 $\mu$m), narrow (3 $\mu$m) and near-bamboo Al-Cu lines. Earlier random-microstructure simulations fixed the Cu effect, and most continuum solute models used prescribed structures with all Cu mobile; we join the two and let only dissolved Cu move, the excess staying as immobile precipitate. With $10^3$ maps per condition at 200 $^\circ$C, we report nucleation times and sites, immortal fractions and Black's-law parameters. In cathode-limited lines Cu depletion shortens the median nucleation time 1.15- to 1.20-fold relative to immobile Cu for the cited Cu diffusivity; treating all Cu as mobile overstates this a further 2.24- to 2.36-fold at 2 wt%; the exponent stays near 2. In wide lines with reservoir ends, Cu steps left by the immobile precipitate become the failure sites, removing their Cu-free immortality if a quarter or more of the precipitate blocks Al diffusion. With Cu above the solvus the lifetime is not Arrhenius, as the dissolved fraction rises with temperature: a Black's-law fit at 250 to 300 $^\circ$C overestimates the median nucleation time at 150 $^\circ$C up to 2.33-fold at 2 wt% Cu with an exponent near 2. At the test durations the model qualitatively matches the cathode-side damage in narrow lines and the Cu build-up and mid-line peaks in wide lines but not the cathode-side Cu peaks or longer lives of narrow lines.

### Paper [142]
Title: A Fractional Chern Semimetal: Exact Wavefunction and Conformal Criticality
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31234
Authors: Fa-Jie Wang, Yang Qi, Zhi-Da Song
Abstract: Unlike fractional quantum Hall states in uniform Landau levels, fractional Chern insulators can host dispersive anyon bands, raising the question of what happens when the anyon gap closes. We construct an exact finite-bond-dimension tensor-network state for a fractional Chern semimetal by Gutzwiller projecting a critical parton state. This state exhibits divergent correlation lengths in charged sectors but a fractionally quantized many-body Chern number of $1/3$. Although it has one electron per unit cell, its modular $S$ and $T$ matrices and entanglement spectrum match those of the $\nu=1/3$ Laughlin state. We identify a $(1+1)$--dimensional $U(3)_1\times SU(3)_1$ conformal field theory (CFT) governing the wavefunction norm in the infrared, an unexpected result for a state with $U(1)_3$ topology. The $SU(3)_1$ sector is a nonlinear sigma model whose Wess--Zumino level and kinetic stiffness are determined by the parton Chern number and Fubini--Study metric, respectively. Under renormalization, it flows to the conformal fixed point, with an initial distance to this fixed point controlled by the idealness of parton quantum geometry. Favorable geometry enables precise agreement with CFT predictions even on narrow cylinders.

### Paper [143]
Title: Quantum-geometry-tuned Mott transition and emergent heavy fermions in a topological flat band
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31242
Authors: Ya-Hui Zhang
Abstract: A flat band with a nonzero Chern number has no localized Wannier orbitals, which raises the question of whether Mott physics is still possible. We place a Hubbard interaction $U$ on coherent orbitals, which are localized but overcomplete, and develop a single-site dynamical mean-field theory (DMFT) for this model. Within the flat band alone, DMFT reproduces the experimentally observed cascade and inverse-compressibility sawtooth without invoking Dirac revival or remote-band electrons. At nonzero integer filling we find an insulator-to-metal transition tuned by $s^2$, the fraction of the Brillouin zone that carries the Berry curvature. For $N_f=8$ flavors the Mott transition at $\nu=-2$ occurs at $s^2_c\approx0.025$, where the top of the lower Hubbard band reaches the Fermi level and the local moments are Kondo screened, forming a heavy Fermi liquid (HFL). The HFL extends to the hole-doped side, $\nu=-2-x$, but above the small Kondo temperature $T_K$ the state is an incoherent bad metal without a Fermi surface. At low temperature, an anti-Hund's coupling $J_A\sim0.1U$ replaces the HFL with a pseudogap metal that has a small Fermi surface and is dominated by spin-polaron excitations at small $x$. We propose this pseudogap metal as the parent state of superconductivity in TBG. The framework may also be applied to other topological bands, such as those of twisted WSe$_2$.

### Paper [144]
Title: Burgers equation from non-thermal stationary states in nearly-integrable gases
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.09726
Authors: Pawe{\l} Lisiak, Maciej {\L}ebek, Mi{\l}osz Panfil
Abstract: When a gas of particles interacts with much a larger reservoir the density dynamics on large scales is typically governed by diffusion. We study this paradigmatic problem for weakly coupled integrable systems and show that this picture gets altered, when transport is investigated on top of long-lived non-thermal states. Remarkably, for states non-invariant under parity we find Burgers equation arising in the hydrodynamic limit. We explicitly compute the diffusion constant and nonlinear advective coefficient of the Burgers equation using a variant of the Chapman-Enskog theory. We find an excellent agreement between our theory and numerical simulations of a simplified model of stochastic two-body collisions. Our conclusions are based only on Galilean invariance, existence of a small system-bath coupling parameter and a small momentum exchange between the system and the bath particles during two-body scattering.

### Paper [145]
Title: From dynamic strain aging to Alessandro--Beatrice--Bertotti--Montorsi dynamics of propagating Portevin--Le Chatelier bands
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24141
Authors: Tero M\"akinen, Mikko J. Alava
Abstract: Type-A Portevin--Le Chatelier bands are propagating strain localizations whose velocity exhibits strong intermittent fluctuations. We derive a coarse-grained connection between the dynamic-strain-aging model of H\"ahner and Rizzi and the Alessandro--Beatrice--Bertotti--Montorsi (ABBM) mean-field model of avalanche dynamics. A propagating band is reduced to a single phase coordinate while retaining the elastic feedback of the tensile machine. This feedback generates the increasing drive and restoring terms of the ABBM description. A finite-time adjoint calculation determines the stress mobility of the band and is checked independently by direct stress continuation. The mobility is approximately proportional to the hardening parameter, whereas the band speed scales approximately as its square root. Short-range heterogeneity in the plastic strain deposited by the band is converted by the global machine feedback into a Brownian effective force. The resulting reduction provides a microscopic route from dynamic strain aging to ABBM-like avalanche dynamics of propagating PLC bands.

### Paper [146]
Title: Design and performance of a top-loading vibrating-sample magnetometer for a helium-flow variable-temperature insert
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28072
Authors: Alfons G. Schuck, Sebastian K\"olsch, J\"org Franke, Michael Huth
Abstract: We present a custom-built top-loading probe that adds vibrating-sample magnetometry to an existing helium-flow variable-temperature insert without replacing its cryogenic or magnetic-field infrastructure. The modular instrument combines a detachable sealed drive head, a voice-coil resonator operating near 44 Hz, continuous monitoring of the peak vibration amplitude, motorized axial centering, and a compact first-order pickup-coil gradiometer. A numerical flux-linkage calculation reproduces the position and shape of the measured axial response for several vibration amplitudes after multiplication by a common factor of 1.08. Measurements on Ni reference samples demonstrate operation down to 5 K and yield effective geometrical factors between 24.0 V s A$^{-1}$ m$^{-3}$ and 31.4 V s A$^{-1}$ m$^{-3}$. At fixed field, the observed voltage scatter corresponds to moment-equivalent one-standard-deviation repeatabilities of 16.5 nA m$^{2}$ to 27.8 nA m$^{2}$. For a 31.7 nm Ni film measured at 250 K, magnetization curves obtained at three vibration amplitudes approximately collapse after amplitude correction and yield a saturation magnetization of approximately 409 kA m$^{-1}$ after subtraction of the linear high-field background. The results demonstrate a practical retrofit approach for adding bulk and thin-film magnetometry to VTIs with comparable bore and flange constraints.

### Paper [147]
Title: Geometric foundations of mesoscopic nucleation theory: commutativity of kinetic and probabilistic reduction for the Dean-Kawasaki equation
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28163
Authors: Miguel A. Dur\'an-Olivencia
Abstract: The reduction of a stochastic field equation to a few degrees of freedom is a central problem in many areas of physics, applied mathematics and engineering. In this work we present two routes for the dimensional reduction of conservative stochastic partial differential equations of Dean-Kawasaki type. To our knowledge, such reductions have always been carried out at the level of the trajectories, i.e. on Dean's equation, and never at the level of the probability distribution, i.e. on Kawasaki's equation. The first route starts from the parametrisation of the density introduced by Lutsko [J. F. Lutsko, J. Chem. Phys. 136, 034509 (2012)], who imposed it on the field equation and obtained stochastic equations for the order parameters by integrating against a suitable weight. The second route, which reduces Kawasaki's equation, is new. This route averages the functional forward Kolmogorov equation over the fast transverse degrees of freedom, using Fermi tubular coordinates around the manifold. Since the equations of Dean and Kawasaki are formally equivalent, one would expect both reductions to be equivalent as well. We show that this is not the case. In particular, we prove that the two reduced generators coincide if and only if the noise-induced drift corrections arising along each route, which we call anomalies, are equal, and that this condition fails in general. For noise regularised over a correlation length $\delta$ on a spherical domain, the mismatch diverges as $\delta^{-1}$ for the ideal gas and, under a closure of the transverse dynamics, as $\delta^{-3}$ for a fluid with a local excess free energy, the latter order being set by the closure. Finally, we show that, under the same closure, regularity hypotheses and the normalisation $\varepsilon^2 = N^{-1}$, the two reduced theories differ after a time $t$ by at most $O(t/n\delta^3)$, where $n$ is the mean number density.

### Paper [148]
Title: An all-orders proof of gradient flow for multiscalar $\phi^4$ theory in four dimensions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28206
Authors: Marten Reehorst
Abstract: We prove that the renormalisation group flow of multiscalar $\phi^4$ theory in four dimensions is a gradient flow to all orders in perturbation theory. The proof uses the Weyl consistency conditions of Osborn and Jack, and it assumes two properties of perturbation theory: that the generating functional with local couplings is finite (also assumed by Osborn and Jack), and that the known expansion of massive Feynman integrals at large momenta holds with a bound on its remainder at each momentum. The consistency conditions give a gradient formula in which the tensor that multiplies the beta function is allowed to have an antisymmetric part, determined by coefficients of the Weyl anomaly. We derive a Ward identity for the two-point function of the marginal operators at constant couplings away from fixed points, with sources for the currents of field rotations and for the mass operator. It re-expresses one of the anti-symmetric contributions in terms of two-point functions and the first moment of the three-point function. We show that, after this rewrite, each of the resulting terms is equivalent to a symmetric contribution to the metric and a contribution to the potential, and we give both explicitly. The metric is positive definite to all orders in perturbation theory. The proof implies in particular the four relations among five- and six-loop coefficients of the field rotation, which Pannell and Stergiou derived as the conditions for a gradient flow at six loops. We check by explicit computation that these relations indeed hold. A companion paper treats $d=4-\varepsilon$ and other critical dimensions with a Ward identity on the sphere.

### Paper [149]
Title: State-selective dynamical freezing in driven Rydberg atom chains
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28215
Authors: Madhumita Sarkar, Sougato Bose, Roopayan Ghosh
Abstract: Dynamical freezing, the near-complete suppression of dynamics at special drive frequencies, offers a route to mitigating Floquet heating in periodically driven quantum systems. In interacting systems, however, the energy cost of each transition depends on the many-body configuration, so that the freezing condition itself becomes state dependent. We study this dependence in periodically driven Rydberg atom chains under single- and bi-frequency driving. Using Floquet perturbation theory, we show that each spin flip is characterised by a single interference amplitude set by its local interaction shift, and that a state freezes only where the amplitudes of all its available flips vanish simultaneously. This picture accounts quantitatively for the shift and proliferation of freezing frequencies with interatomic spacing and for the response of entire families of initial states, which are sorted by a single local motif. Freezing is strong only within a window of spacings bounded by interaction tails on one side and nearest-neighbour excitation facilitation on the other, outside of which the advantage of bi-frequency driving is lost. In the regime of large detuning amplitude, where freezing points are numerous and the perturbative description is controlled, this window leaves almost no room for the PXP limit.

### Paper [150]
Title: Observation of collective flatbands in hybrid Mie-grating THz metasurfaces
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28246
Authors: Jie Ji, Thanh Xuan Hoang, Ayan Nussupbekov, Siyan Zhou, Kehan Wang, Yuri Kivshar, Jaime G\'omez Rivas
Abstract: We propose a novel type of functional metasurfaces created by a hybridization of Mie resonators with high-index optical gratings. We demonstrate that such hybrid metasurfaces possess flatbands characterized by zero group velocity and strong energy localization. We experimentally observe the flatbands in free-standing terahertz (THz) metasurfaces, where dielectric interconnections between Mie resonators, forming the grating, mediate multipolar hybridization and provide a geometric control over collective resonances. Our THz near-field measurements with a dipole excitation uncover long-lived coherent oscillations associated with the quasi-flat magnetic-dipolar resonant modes, while a spatial mapping resolves the resonant electromagnetic response across the dielectric interconnections. We vary the grating width continuously to tune the hybrid magnetic-dipolar resonances and substantially enhance the quality factor (Q factor), while preserving the quasi-!atband dispersion. Complementary Bloch-resolved Purcell calculations reveal the substantial Purcell enhancement with increasing Q factor, followed by a trend towards saturation and highlighting the interplay between radiative confinement and local emitter-mode coupling. We believe our findings establish the engineering of dielectric interconnections in periodic lattices as a novel strategy for spectrally tuning high-Q flatbands in these metastructures, tailoring localized electromagnetic interactions in functional optical metasurfaces.

### Paper [151]
Title: Dynamics of the Nuclear Envelope and Mitotic Spindle in Closed Mitosis
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28302
Authors: Wilson Lough, Zachary R. Gergely, Eduardo Corona, Meredith D. Betterton
Abstract: During closed mitosis, large-scale deformation of the nuclear envelope is driven by the mitotic spindle and chromosomes contained within it. Previous models of closed mitosis have established that the spatial distribution of spindle-generated forces influences nuclear morphology, but have imposed axisymmetry and focused on equilibrium shapes. We introduce a three-dimensional biophysical model of nuclear envelope remodeling and spindle elongation that incorporates thermal fluctuations to examine how spindle-generated forces, chromatin distribution, and nuclear envelope elasticity control nuclear shape in fission yeast. The model reproduces features of both successful and aberrant nuclear shape change, including centering of mispositioned spindles, the dumbbell nuclear shape transition, and the formation of nuclear protrusions. A phase diagram of nuclear shape shows that insufficient spindle force produces elongated but undivided nuclei, while sufficiently large spindle forces produce either symmetric division or envelope tethers, depending on the size of the chromatin-envelope contact region. Near the boundary between symmetric division and tethering, nuclei develop asymmetric single-tether configurations. A reduced-order representation of the coupled envelope-spindle system shows a symmetry-breaking transition between symmetric and asymmetric nuclear shapes. Below a critical chromatin-envelope contact size, symmetric configurations lose stability during spindle elongation and thermal fluctuations drive nuclear shape trajectories toward one of two stable asymmetric configurations. These findings identify a growth-driven mechanical mechanism for spindle centering and alignment during early mitosis and reveal how asymmetric nuclear morphologies can arise during anaphase even in the absence of structural differences between the two spindle poles.

### Paper [152]
Title: Cryogenic Luminescence Thermometry of Upconversion Micro- and Nanoparticles via Deep Learning
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28311
Authors: Pablo Jos\'e Coria Ledo, Lina W\"olcken, Lucas R. Mendicino, Santiago Gliosca, M. Claudia Marchi, Marcos Illescas, Beatriz Barja, Christian Tom\'as Schmiegelow
Abstract: Luminescence thermometry with single or optically trapped particles is hampered by weak signals that fluctuate with alignment, collection efficiency, and excitation conditions and, for $\mathrm{Er}^{3+}$-based probes, by the loss of sensitivity of the luminescence intensity ratio (LIR) at cryogenic temperatures. Here we use a one-dimensional convolutional neural network (1D-CNN) to predict the temperature directly from single emission spectra of $\mathrm{NaYF}_4{:}\mathrm{Yb}^{3+},\mathrm{Er}^{3+}$ micro- and nanoparticles between $-150\,^\circ\mathrm{C}$ and $105\,^\circ\mathrm{C}$, and compare it with the standard LIR under adverse conditions deliberately reproduced with particle ensembles. From $-150\,^\circ\mathrm{C}$ to $30\,^\circ\mathrm{C}$, the 1D-CNN reduces the single-spectrum mean absolute error from $5.4\,^\circ\mathrm{C}$ to $1.0\,^\circ\mathrm{C}$ and keeps its temperature uncertainty nearly constant down to the lowest temperatures, where Stark-component features remain temperature dependent while the LIR loses its sensitivity. Under deliberate optical misalignment, its error increases only from $0.58\,^\circ\mathrm{C}$ to $0.88\,^\circ\mathrm{C}$, whereas that of the LIR rises from $1.4\,^\circ\mathrm{C}$ to $3.5\,^\circ\mathrm{C}$. The same approach reaches sub-degree accuracy ($0.21\,^\circ\mathrm{C}$) over a narrow temperature window and a single calibration valid across different pump powers and wavelengths. These results establish 1D-CNNs as robust single-shot thermometers and a step toward fast thermometry of single and trapped particles.

### Paper [153]
Title: MatHarness: Towards Long-Horizon Open-ended AutoResearch for Computational Materials Science
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28324
Authors: Yanlin Fei, Lei Li, Shaolong Chen, Nazhou Liu, Xinmiao Yu, Madalina Ciobanu, Navan Preet Singh, Qingqing Mao, Ritankar Das
Abstract: Materials science research is a non-linear, long-horizon process. Existing scientific agents, however, largely rely on specific task execution or predefined workflows, rendering them brittle in open-ended settings. To bridge this gap, we present MatHarness, an agent harness designed for open-ended, long-horizon computational materials research spanning the complete lifecycle from initial ideation to manuscript writing. MatHarness couples three mechanisms: execution robustness through pre-flight verification, expert-distilled skills, and just-in-time auditing; epistemic adaptability through persistent research artifacts and an evolving scientific mental model; and collective intelligence through a heterogeneous multi-agent round table under targeted human oversight. We evaluate MatHarness on adsorbate-induced surface segregation in Cu-Zn alloys, an open question in methanol-synthesis catalysis. Over a multi-week trajectory consuming roughly 10^3 GPU-hours (50 production DFT relaxations prescreened by 171 machine-learning-potential evaluations) the system revised its plan six times, recovered autonomously from failed calculations, and had an independent auditor overturn one of its own over-attributed claims before publication. Within the sampled metallic dilute-limit models, the validated DFT analysis supports an adsorbate-induced tendency toward greater surface Cu exposure at both composition endpoints, with facet- and coverage-dependent exceptions. These results were compiled into a complete manuscript with human involvement concentrated in specification, approval, and strategic steering gates. The code is publicly available at https://github.com/PrentisAI/pippin.

### Paper [154]
Title: MagTrace: Tracing magnetic order through the evolving scientific literature
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28338
Authors: Mengnan Sun, Cheng Peng
Abstract: Much of the research on magnetic materials starts from their magnetic order, but confirming it from the papers by hand can take hours, and curated databases can be incomplete and outdated. In this work, we build MagTrace, a language model agent that, given only a material's name, searches four literature databases, reads open access full text and reports the magnetic orders the current literature supports, each with a confidence score and citations a reader can check. We evaluate its performance on 18 materials against an answer key complied from published papers, compared with two other conditions that vary in level of retrieval. On a score from 0 to 1 that combines correctness, detail, whether the most confident order is current, and citation integrity, MagTrace scores 0.839; the same underlying language model scores 0.676 given the abstracts from one literature search and 0.548 answering from memory. With a single search, the model finds the right kind of order but omits the propagation vector, which describes how the pattern repeats through the material's crystal structure, in 85% of its orders. From memory, the model often cites titles that lead to no indexed paper (for 25% of the orders we could check), and when a cited paper exists and has an abstract, the abstract contradicts the claim for 17% of those citations, against 7% for MagTrace.

### Paper [155]
Title: Integrable Orbital Exchange between Ellipsoidally Deformed $p$, $d$, and $f$ Electron States: Two-Angle Sklyanin Representations and Fused Elliptic $R$-Matrices
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28444
Authors: Vladimir Mikhalev
Abstract: We construct integrable orbital exchange models from ellipsoidally deformed $p$, $d$, and $f$ multiplets. A two-angle realization of the Sklyanin algebra gives explicit Jacobi difference operators $D,L_i$, complete distortion spectra and eigenfunctions, and a positive inner product transported from Sklyanin's integral. In the $p$ sector the coordinate functions carry an exact spin-one action, and applying $D$ componentwise maps the confocal ellipsoid to a sphere. Elliptic fusion gives explicit $R$-matrices in the angular generators on both factors. Their Yang--Baxter relations yield commuting charges and exchange Hamiltonians for homogeneous and alternating chains, as well as mixed-pair interactions that can act between angular degrees of freedom on the same center. Exchange changes angular projections and correlations while preserving the two local Casimirs of each constituent, which fix its angular-momentum-squared and distortion-plus-rotation kinetic-energy invariants. The $p$--$p$ model recovers Fateev's elliptic spin-one interaction and reduces to the Takhtajan--Babujian Hamiltonian in the rational isotropic limit.

### Paper [156]
Title: Distinguishing photothermal and photoelectrokinetic contributions to flow in light-activated microchannel architectures
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28481
Authors: Sudharshan Anandan, Yuhang Fang, Lily Farmer, Maria Irineu, Shanmukhi Sripada, Tyler Hughes, Jared Nathan, Sathish Rajendran, Steven Wereley, Amy Marconnet, Thomas Beechem, Jeffrey L. Moran, David M. Warsinger
Abstract: Light-driven flows near Janus surfaces could power microfluidic devices without external hardware. These flows are often attributed to photoinduced electro- or diffusio-osmosis, but illumination also heats the surface, potentially driving natural convection. Here, we map flow and temperature around a UVA-illuminated TiO$_2$-Au Janus membrane using depth-resolved particle tracking and infrared thermometry. Recirculating rolls form on both sides, with no measurable net flow across the membrane. Their cubic, reversing velocity profile identifies horizontal (Hadley) convection, driven by a lateral temperature gradient as the gold film absorbs light. The measured profile agrees with wall-corrected Hadley theory using one fitted parameter. Adding 100 mM NaCl to suppress electrokinetic flows leaves the profile unchanged, indicating that convection dominates. Simulations show that convection falls below 1 $\mu$m/s in thin channels, where surface-driven osmotic flows could dominate. Thus, device geometry and surface chemistry determine which mechanism governs light-driven microfluidic flows.

### Paper [157]
Title: Coherent Control of Single-Photon Emission in Broadband Plasmonic Resonators
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28493
Authors: Athul S. Rema, Adri\'an E. Rubio L\'opez, Felipe Herrera
Abstract: Encoding phase information with quantum emitters in metallic optical nanocavities is challenging due to ultrafast relaxation induced by the broadband nature of the plasmonic near field. Using macroscopic quantum electrodynamics theory, we demonstrate that an individual emitter strongly coupled to a plasmonic nanocavity field that is driven by a pulsed laser with frequency comb spectrum undergoes spontaneous emission of well-resolved one-photon pulses whose amplitude scales quadratically with the number of frequency comb components due to coherent accumulation, and ultrashort non-exponential temporal profiles with duration set by the light-matter coupling strength. Conditions are discussed for exponentially suppressing the probability of two or more emission events within the lifetime of the coupled emitter-nanocavity system. The spectral signatures of the optimal one-photon wavepackets are discussed. This work paves the way for the development of ultrafast laser-triggered single-photon emitters using atoms and molecules in optical nanocavities.

### Paper [158]
Title: From atomic events to material degradation across metre and year scales
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28559
Authors: Kun Li, Tian Luo, Haozhi Han, Ruge Zhang, Yifeng Chen, Xinfu He, Yunquan Zhang, Ting Cao, Yunxin Liu
Abstract: Material degradation emerges from microscopic events whose consequences accumulate over years, yet mechanism-resolved simulations remain confined below engineering timescales. The challenge is not just the sheer number of events: atomistic evolution is dominated by recurrent local motion, with only a tiny fraction driving lasting structural change. This distinction is not encoded in an instantaneous atomic configuration. Locally indistinguishable states can exhibit different event competition, escape times, and long-horizon outcomes, revealing missing kinetic context as a critical information bottleneck. This context spans space and time: the future consequence of a local transition depends on its surrounding defect environment and the kinetic history leading to its current state.
