To solve this, AtomWorld reconstructs a predictive kinetic state from multiscale structural observations and trajectory history, distinguishing transient fluctuations from microscopic pathways that drive persistent structural evolution. Learning operates only over transitions admitted by the underlying kinetic Monte Carlo (KMC) process, preserving event legality, state evolution, and physical time. Across BCC Fe-Cu precipitation, FCC Ni-Cr-Al ordering, and HCP Zr-H interstitial transport, restoring kinetic context consistently advances persistent evolution while maintaining physical fidelity and transferring to unseen conditions. In irradiated reactor-pressure-vessel steel, this approach achieves up to 420-fold greater persistent evolution under matched event budgets. Combined with scalable execution, it successfully extends mechanism-resolved trajectories to service-year timescales across metre-scale heterogeneous domains.

### Paper [159]
Title: Metastable states are precursors of Turing patterns in Adaptive Networks
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28828
Authors: S. Nirmala Jenifer, Timoteo Carletti
Abstract: In networked reaction-diffusion systems, emergence of patterns from a homogeneous equilibrium can be described by Turing instability. Recently, Turing theory has been developed to include adaptive networks where, i.e., link weights change as a function of the states of the nodes. In this work we show that metastable states, defined as long living transient epochs with unique dynamical properties, could be observed in a range of parameters between Turing patterns and absence of any spatial pattern. Stated differently in the metastable regime the system ''switches'' between the homogeneous solution and the Turing pattern, never settling in any of those states, i.e., alternating silent and spiking regimes. By using phase diagrams and recurrence plots, we have numerically shown the existence of metastable states for the Brusselator model. Moreover, the dependence of the emergence of the metastable states on the adaptive control parameter has been characterized. The applicability of methods and ideas proposed in this work go beyond the considered case, they can help us in understanding conditions under which metastable states arise and could thus be informative to predict and control the latter.

### Paper [160]
Title: Systematically improvable semiclassical clustering for accurate and efficient quantum dynamics in large spin ensembles
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28958
Authors: Pranay Venkatesh, Andr\'es Montoya-Castillo
Abstract: Predicting the decoherence of spin qubits in realistic environments is key to identifying the interactions that limit their coherence---a prerequisite for designing better quantum technologies. While accurate methods for spin dynamics are limited to relatively small systems, the dissipative spin environments in real experiments typically involve orders of magnitude more spins. Semiclassical methods can reach these sizes, but their classical treatment of the bath cannot capture the sensor-bath entanglement that generates coherence revivals, especially under the application of refocusing pulses. Here, we resolve this tension. We introduce cluster spin-LSC, a method that clusters the sensor together with a few strongly coupled bath spins, treating them quantum mechanically in an otherwise classical spin bath. We show that our method is applicable to systems containing arbitrary spin species under dynamical decoupling pulse sequences. Our method converges systematically to the exact dynamics with cluster size, providing an internal consistency check when no exact benchmarks exist. We demonstrate that cluster spin-LSC recovers numerically exact coherence and population dynamics for a nitrogen-vacancy center and a molecular qubit, reducing day-long multi-GPU simulations to only minutes on a CPU. We further extend our method to systems with large spin baths that lie beyond the reach of exact methods. Our approach thus offers a scalable and systematically improvable route to accurately simulate the decoherence dynamics of sensors in realistic spin environments.

### Paper [161]
Title: Extensive-Rank Matrix Denoising Under i.i.d. Non-Gaussian Noise
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29179
Authors: Zhuohang He, Qun Chen, Junjie Ma, Haochuan Zhang, Xiaojun Yuan
Abstract: We study extensive-rank matrix denoising for rotationally invariant signals corrupted by i.i.d. non-Gaussian noise. To exploit both the signal spectrum and the entrywise noise prior, we consider an orthogonal approximate message passing (OAMP) algorithm that alternates between a rotationally invariant spectral estimator and an entrywise denoiser. At each OAMP iteration, we conjecture a high-dimensional Gaussian equivalence property: the entrywise module admits an effective additive scalar Gaussian channel, whereas the spectral module behaves as if corrupted by i.i.d. Gaussian noise of matching variance. Under this conjecture, we formulate a state evolution (SE) recursion for the effective noise variances of both modules. Numerical simulations confirm that the SE predictions closely track the empirical mean-square errors. For robust principal component analysis (PCA), we apply this OAMP algorithm and characterize its recovery phase diagram via SE. Numerical results show that OAMP achieves a larger recovery region than several existing methods.

### Paper [162]
Title: Polarization-independent self-hybridized strong coupling by Brillouin zone folding induced quasi-bound states in the continuum in bulk WS2 metasurfaces
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29204
Authors: Xiongwei Guo, Fu Liu, Jinhai Si, Xun Hou, Kuidong Wang
Abstract: High-refractive-index dielectric metasurfaces supporting optical modes are ideal candidates for achieving strong coupling with transition-metal dichalcogenides (TMDCs) materials. However, optical illumination with a specific polarization is always required to estimate the maximal coupling strength in such coupled systems due to the polarization-dependent optical modes. Here, we propose a self-hybridized strong coupling between Brillouin zone folding induced quasi-bound states in the continuum (BZF-QBICs) mode and A excitons in the same bulk WS2 metasurface. By changing the thickness of the bulk WS2 metasurfaces, a typical anticrossing behavior with Rabi splitting of 165.9 meV is observed. Such Rabi splitting is unchanged when changing the polarization of the incident light from linearly to circularly polarizations, demonstrating the polarization-independent self-hybridized strong coupling. This work provides a novel method for realizing robust polarization-independent polaritons in bulk TMDCs metasurface and offers new insights for expanding the applications of polaritonic devices with arbitrary polarizations.

### Paper [163]
Title: A Novel Algebraic Approach to Medium-Range Solvable Models
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29351
Authors: Yuan Miao, Kohei Fukai
Abstract: We extend Jones' Baxterisation, which produces solutions to the Yang-Baxter equation directly from the generators of an algebra, to quantum spin chains with medium-range interactions. The key ingredient is an extended Yang-Baxter equation for R matrices that are built from the generators attached to two neighbouring sites. Together with the accompanying Lax operator, it gives rise to transfer matrices that are in involution up to boundary terms, exactly as in Jones' construction. We find a solution to the extended Yang-Baxter equation, of non-difference form, in terms of the generators of the chromatic algebra of 3-colouring, and obtain from it two families of medium-range lattice Hamiltonians. Thus, each representation of the algebra provides a medium-range solvable model. Using the relation between the chromatic algebra of 3-colouring and the fusion category $\mathrm{Rep}(S_3)$, we construct four such representations. They reproduce the folded XXZ model and the hard-rod deformed XXZ model, and give new medium-range models of a three-state antiferromagnet and of a Rydberg-blockade ladder. Two of the four models possess non-invertible symmetries.

### Paper [164]
Title: Dynamic Renewal for Hydrodynamic Coupling in Ciliary Mucus Transport
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29378
Authors: Jeroen Bechtold, Valentijn van Zwieten, Iris Verkuil, Meike F. Bos, Georg Rempfer, Mae Nesenberend, Pieter Michels, Florian K. R. Gaeremynck, Bart Stam, Debabrata Panja, Joost de Graaf
Abstract: Mucociliary clearance in large mammalian airways involves millimetric MUC5B-rich mucus strands that emerge from submucosal glands approximately aligned with the direction of transport. These strands are observed downstream in cross-flow networks. Recently, Bos et al. [PNAS Nexus 2, pgad388 (2023)] modeled single strand reorientation using a free-draining coupling for the flow induced by cilia. This leaves unresolved whether the strand's own disturbance of the surrounding liquid assists or frustrates the turning. Here, we develop the methodology to address this question. An immersed-boundary coupling is combined with a Fourier-space Stokes solver and a bespoke dynamic-renewal algorithm that exposes the strand to new ciliary domain without enlarging the simulation volume. This allows us to overcome one of the multiscale challenges posed by mucociliary clearance. We find that hydrodynamic interactions result in only a limited decrease in the rate of reorientation, which supports the use of the free-draining approximation. Future work can build on the methodological foundation introduced here to study collective strand dynamics.

### Paper [165]
Title: Nonempirical Determination of the Optimal TAO-DFT Fictitious Temperature for Molecular Systems in the Lowest Singlet State
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29414
Authors: Bing-Ying Li, Jeng-Da Chai
Abstract: Thermally-assisted-occupation density functional theory (TAO-DFT) [J.-D. Chai, J. Chem. Phys., 2012, ${\bf 136}$, 154104] has been developed to investigate the ground-state properties of large electronic systems exhibiting varying multi-reference (MR) character. As a central parameter in TAO-DFT, the fictitious temperature $\theta$ is closely linked to the degree of static correlation in an electronic system. While the conventional system-independent $\theta$-scheme is computationally efficient and broadly applicable, a fixed, preassigned $\theta$ can be overly large for single-reference (SR) systems yet too small for strongly MR systems. To overcome this limitation, we propose a nonempirical scheme based on spin-symmetry restoration to determine the optimal system-dependent fictitious temperature $\theta^{*}$ for molecular systems in their lowest singlet states. Across benchmark tests encompassing both MR systems (the bond dissociation of H$_{2}$, N$_{2}$, Li$_{2}$, and LiH, as well as the torsional rotation of twisted ethylene) and pure SR systems (the dissociation of He$_{2}$ and Ne$_{2}$), our nonempirical approach demonstrates superior overall accuracy compared with the conventional fixed-$\theta$ scheme. Furthermore, relative to standard Kohn-Sham density functional theory (TAO-DFT with $\theta = 0$), TAO-DFT employing $\theta^{*}$ yields substantial improvements for MR systems while preserving comparable accuracy in SR systems.

### Paper [166]
Title: Scaling of phase rigidity without eigenstate coalescence
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29463
Authors: Shang-En Xie, Li-Mei Chen, Yao Zhou, Peng Ye
Abstract: Phase rigidity vanishes as a power law near exceptional points, reflecting the coalescence of eigenstates at these points. In this Letter, we show that non-Hermitian skin effects induce distinct asymptotic behaviors of phase rigidity without eigenstate coalescence. To demonstrate this, we derive exact analytical results for three non-Hermitian chain models and verify them numerically. For the ordinary skin effect, phase rigidity decays exponentially with increasing system size $N$, as the right and left eigenstates are exponentially localized at opposite boundaries. For the scale-free skin effect, phase rigidity exhibits an $N^{-2}$ power-law approach to a nonzero constant as the right and left eigenstates have decay lengths proportional to $N$. For the algebraic skin effect studied here, phase rigidity can exhibit an $N^{-1/2}$ power-law decay to zero, with the right eigenstate algebraically localized near a boundary and the left eigenstate spread across the chain. These findings show that non-Hermitian skin effects govern phase-rigidity scaling by shaping the spatial profiles of the right and left eigenstates, enabling control of eigenstate nonorthogonality without exceptional points.

### Paper [167]
Title: Stable eigenmodes of the Large-scale circulation in Turbulent Convection
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29636
Authors: Yin Wang, Pik-Yin Lai, Penger Tong
Abstract: Turbulent Rayleigh-B\'enard convection (RBC) is a canonical system for studying buoyancy-driven turbulence. Its defining feature is a system-size large-scale circulation (LSC), whose dynamics reflect the coupling between coherent motion and turbulent fluctuations. Starting from the Boussinesq Navier-Stokes and temperature equations, we derive three coupled stochastic equations for the LSC angular velocity and its associated dipolar temperature field. The deterministic dynamics contain an unstable no-LSC set and two symmetry-related stable finite-LSC states. Linearization about either finite-LSC state yields one purely relaxational eigenmode and a pair of damped oscillatory eigenmodes whose frequency is set by the LSC turnover rate. Turbulent fluctuations continually excite the latter, providing a dynamical origin for the observed quasi-periodic oscillations. Experiments in a thin-disk cell directly resolve both eigenmodes and show their predicted Rayleigh-number scalings. The same stochastic dynamics also provide a route to rare cessations and reversals. This theory-experiment framework provides a tractable example of how coherent circulation states, intrinsic modes, and fluctuation-driven transitions emerge in turbulent convection, with broader relevance to geophysical and astrophysical flows.

### Paper [168]
Title: A Theoretical Investigation of Magnetic Susceptibility Measurement of Diamagnetic Liquids Using a Michelson Interferometer
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29671
Authors: David Shulman
Abstract: We examine the theoretical use of a Michelson interferometer to infer the magnetic susceptibility of a diamagnetic liquid from a magnetically induced surface deformation. A ring magnet changes the liquid thickness along the sample beam, producing a signed phase shift relative to a fixed optical reference. For a double traversal of the sample, the fringe displacement is $m = 2(n_{\ell} - n_a)\Delta z/\lambda$. We derive the susceptibility inversion both in a local gravity approximation and with surface tension included through the linearized Young-Laplace equation. The treatment distinguishes a finite reference field from a zero-field reference, specifies the displacement sign and susceptibility contrast, and identifies calibration and uncertainty requirements. Reproducible illustrative calculations use a uniformly magnetized annulus. The results establish a theoretical measurement relation and its assumptions; they do not constitute an experimental demonstration or a verified accuracy claim.

### Paper [169]
Title: Coarse-Grained Description of Stochastic Neutron Transport in Highly Heterogeneous Reactor Media via Non-Local Feller Boundary Functionals
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29680
Authors: V. V. Ryazanov
Abstract: This paper presents a novel hierarchical coarse-graining framework to model non-Markovian neutron transport within the ultra-heterogeneous, multi-phase subcooled nucleate boiling layer of a nuclear reactor core. By applying Feller's subordination theory to continuous-time random walks, microscopic fluid fluctuations and vapor bubble dynamics are rigorously integrated into macroscopic transport operators through the apparatus of Complete Bernstein Functions (CBF). A modified non-local Fick's law is derived using a $P_1$-Legendre polynomial expansion, mapping the fractal porosity and structural phase boundaries into a stochastically renormalized effective diffusion coefficient. The thermodynamic consistency of the model is grounded within Chentsov's statistical manifolds, demonstrating that granular Poisson-like shot noise yields a geometric-exponential family of distributions characterized by a constant negative information curvature. To evaluate operational safety margins under transient conditions, a generalized Lyapunov functional with a quadratic memory kernel is constructed, and a complex decomposition of the transfer function $H(\omega)$ is performed. The resulting analytical limits are parameterized for a WWER-1000 core layout and translated into an engineering computational sequence suitable for implementation in Mathcad Prime 8 to diagnose the onset of Hopf bifurcations and spatial power oscillations.

### Paper [170]
Title: Local redundancies are necessary for long-lived memories in finite spatial dimensions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29689
Authors: Chao Yin, Yaodong Li, Benedikt Placke, Vedika Khemani
Abstract: We prove a no-go theorem for exponentially long-lived thermal memories, based on the structure of redundancies among Hamiltonian terms. For local stabilizer Hamiltonians in finite spatial dimensions, the absence of local redundancies---relations in which the product of a small number of Hamiltonian terms equals the identity---is known to preclude bulk thermodynamic phase transitions. Yet glassy dynamics can still sustain long-lived memories even without a thermodynamic transition, as demonstrated by LDPC codes on non-Euclidean graphs. A longstanding question, exemplified by Haah's cubic code, is whether such a mechanism can also support passive (quantum) memory in finite-dimensional Euclidean systems. We show that this route fails for local stabilizer Hamiltonians without local redundancies: at any constant nonzero temperature, local heat-bath dynamics is fast mixing, with $O(N)$ mixing time, where $N$ is the number of qubits. We establish this by proving a strong decay of correlations property of the thermal Gibbs state. We further prove rapid mixing with $O(\log N)$ mixing time under a stronger condition on induced redundancies in suitably chosen regions. This stronger result applies to several well-studied examples, including Haah's cubic code and its generalizations, as well as three-dimensional layer codes. Our work identifies redundancy structure as a key property for understanding thermal memories.

### Paper [171]
Title: Grammar Sparsity Sets How Transformers Learn Random Languages
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29692
Authors: Edgar Andres Hernandez Moreno, Francesco Cagnetta
Abstract: We study language acquisition by transformers trained on the Random Language Model: a synthetic hierarchical grammar with random production rules, whose sparsity is controlled by a single parameter and whose conditional entropies are known exactly. On depth-two grammars, we find that sparsity sets both the order and the speed of learning. Dense grammars are learnt one level at a time: a token is predicted from its sibling after $t\propto V$ steps and from the full context only after $t\propto V^{3}$, so the two stages are separated by a plateau that widens with $V$. Sparse grammars are learnt all at once, at $t\propto\sqrt V$, with a smaller exponent than even the first stage of a dense one. The exponents $1$ and $1/2$ already appear at depth one, where we derive them for a linear model trained with Adam.

### Paper [172]
Title: Selecting Stable Sampling Dynamics for Compositional Diffusion: A Feynman-Kac Weight Reduction
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29764
Authors: Sunjin Kim, Sungsoo Ahn, Michael Chertkov
Abstract: Given $K$ separately trained diffusion models, how should one sample from a $\beta_k$-weighted product of their terminal laws without jointly retraining the experts? Existing Feynman--Kac constructions provide many weighted stochastic differential equations (SDEs) with the correct target marginal. The consequential question is how to select useful proposal dynamics among them. We study an explicit family of weighted diffusions that realizes the same product-of-intermediate-experts trajectory. A scalar parameter $\lambda$ controls how strongly the proposal drift follows the composed expert score, while an additional Langevin component can be varied independently. Writing $B=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\beta_k>0$, we identify the distinguished choice $\lambda=1/B$. At this value, the aggregate squared-score term in the instantaneous Feynman--Kac log-weight rate cancels exactly. For positive expert exponents, the remaining score-disagreement contribution is non-positive. We call this structural cancellation Feynman--Kac reduction. Under bounded-divergence assumptions, it yields an upper bound on the instantaneous unnormalized log-weight rate for all $\lambda\leq 1/B$. For linear diffusion protocols with compactly supported expert data, $\lambda=1/B$ yields two-sided bounds at every nonterminal time, for exponents of either sign. A corresponding result holds for a single expert under the same divergence assumption. In these two-sided settings, the selected value is unique within the specified family in a universal worst-case sense. Experiments on Gaussian mixtures and molecular sampling compare the analytically predicted stability with actual sampling quality, including instances in which parameter choices different from $\lambda=1/B$ perform better empirically.

### Paper [173]
Title: Characterizing Ultrafast Processes via Work
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29778
Authors: Bernard Derrida, Michael Entov, Leonid Polterovich, Lenya Ryzhik
Abstract: Consider the following cycle for a mean-field system initially in thermodynamic equilibrium: an instantaneous parameter jump, relaxation to the new equilibrium, and a reverse jump. For certain systems and parameter values, the two equilibrium macrostates coincide in the thermodynamic limit. We characterize this matching phenomenon through the work required to perform the cycle. Our main result concerns scalar many-particle mean-field systems under suitable assumptions. In the matching regime, the total work has a non-Gaussian limiting distribution, and the average work per particle tends to zero. Away from matching, the properly rescaled fluctuations of the total work have a Gaussian limiting distribution, and the average work per particle converges to a strictly positive constant. Further motivation arises from a contact-geometric model of thermodynamics, in which matching corresponds to a Reeb chord between two equilibrium Legendrian submanifolds.

### Paper [174]
Title: Electric polarizabilities with phaseless auxiliary-field quantum Monte Carlo
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29858
Authors: Leon Otis, Jo Kurian, James Shee
Abstract: While projector quantum Monte Carlo methods are known for producing accurate ground-state energies, the prediction of other properties that encode the electronic response to applied electric and magnetic fields has historically been challenging. In this work we demonstrate that the electric (static) polarizability tensor, which is formally a second-derivative of the energy with respect to electric fields, can be efficiently computed with phaseless auxiliary-field quantum Monte Carlo using a correlated sampling finite-difference strategy. With simple Hartree-Fock trial wave functions, we investigate a series of small molecules (some of which are difficult for approximate density functionals), polyacenes of increasing size, and ferrocene as a representative organometallic complex. The methodology is scalable to relatively large molecules involving thousands of basis functions, and with appropriate trial wave functions remains robust for strongly correlated electronic states, as we show for the stretched dinitrogen molecule. This work opens the door to the computation of a host of other second-order molecular properties with auxiliary-field quantum Monte Carlo, which will inform the ongoing search for optimal trial wave functions and extend benchmark-quality training data sets for the refinement of semi-empirical and machine learning models for quantum chemistry and molecular simulation.

### Paper [175]
Title: Pseudo-Altermagnetism in Non-Hermitian Systems
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29976
Authors: Chang-Yin Ji, Runze Li, Zhi-Ming Yu, Yugui Yao
Abstract: Altermagnetism has attracted growing attention, and its analogs have been actively explored across classical platforms. Here, we introduce pseudo-altermagnetism in non-Hermitian systems, where balanced gain and loss plays the role of the altermagnetic spin, and the opposite gain and loss sublattices are related by an antiunitary symmetry $O$ with $O\bm k\neq \bm k$ for generic momentum $\bm k$. Using a two-dimensional non-Hermitian system with $O=C_{4z}\mathcal{T}$ symmetry as a representative example, we find that pseudo-altermagnetism not only leads to a momentum-dependent gain and loss distribution analogous to the momentum-dependent spin splitting of altermagnets, but also exhibits intrinsic non-Hermitian properties. The bands of $C_{4z}\mathcal{T}$-invariant non-Hermitian systems are either isolated or paired by $C_{4z}\mathcal{T}$, and both cases are guaranteed to host movable but unremovable nodal structures, characterized by the zeros of two distinct quantities. An isolated band exhibits two movable but unremovable Hermitian spectral lines, whose generic points are not invariant under $C_{4z}\mathcal{T}$, in sharp contrast to the conventional Hermitian spectra enforced only at the fixed points of an antiunitary symmetry. The paired bands, in turn, feature a movable but unremovable nodal network formed by the union of real-part and imaginary-part degeneracy lines, whose intersections are generally exceptional points. We further demonstrate these findings in a non-Hermitian photonic crystal. Our results establish a symmetry-based bridge between non-Hermitian physics and altermagnetism, and provide novel mechanisms of Hermitian spectra beyond $\mathcal{PT}$ symmetry.

### Paper [176]
Title: The response field approach to path integral control
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30044
Authors: George Stamatescu
Abstract: This paper investigates non-linear path integral control problems within the response field formalism of nonequilibrium field theory. The aim of this work is exploratory, based on the identification of correspondences between backward adjoint processes and the response field of non-equilibrium statistical physics, whose properties have not been set out in an optimal control setting. The response field is found to play an analogous role to the backward adjoint process under the stochastic maximum principle. In particular, the gradient of the optimal cost-to-go at the initial time, which is proportional to the optimal control, is given by the average of the response field at the initial time. In contrast to the case of non-equilibrium dynamics, the response function is found to be acausal in the control setting. The linear-quadratic case of path integral control is studied, confirming it as the free theory, meaning that solving the control problem is equivalent to completing the square and determining the propagator for a two point boundary value problem. The developments presented here are a necessary prelude to applications based on field theoretic approximation, for both path integral control and their dual inference problems.

### Paper [177]
Title: Diagnostic Accuracy Is Not Deployment Value: Decision-Aligned Reliability Evaluation for Foundation MLIPs in Materials Discovery
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30066
Authors: Harry Sevi, Joonatan E. M. Laulainen
Abstract: In AI-guided materials discovery, machine-learning models increasingly decide which candidate materials should receive expensive first-principles or experimental evaluation. Standard reliability metrics such as uncertainty, calibration, novelty, and ranking accuracy evaluate properties of the predictions, but success on these metrics does not necessarily imply a better downstream screening decision. We study this mismatch for foundation machine-learned interatomic potentials (MACE-MP, CHGNet, Orb V3) used to rank candidate crystal structures before DFT verification. We evaluate three representative reliability interventions (novelty detection, sparse DFT calibration, and chemistry-level ranking audits) using a deployment objective that measures how much DFT can be avoided while maintaining a required recall of near-hull candidates. Across all three, a reliability signal can succeed on its immediate target without improving the final screening decision: novelty detects distribution shift but weakly ranks failure severity; calibration can improve pooled ranking metrics while leaving screening unchanged or degrading it; and sparse audits identify MLIP-chemistry combinations with poor ranking reliability without yielding a robustly beneficial routing policy. A two-oracle analysis further shows that the source of failure depends on the screening decision itself: under formation-energy screening, even perfect knowledge of average ranking reliability can select the wrong model, whereas in the evaluated hull-aligned variant the oracle target becomes decision-aligned but finite-budget audits still fail to realize the value. Reliability should be evaluated relative to the decision it supports: the relevant question is not whether a model is uncertain or inaccurate, but whether acquiring and acting on additional information will improve the final discovery decision enough to justify its cost.

### Paper [178]
Title: Reconstructing Pure-State OTOCs from Forward-Time Linear Response
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30068
Authors: Yu-Hang Jin, Xudong He, Yu Chen
Abstract: We show that the connected out-of-time-ordered correlator (OTOC) of any pure state can be reconstructed exactly from commutator linear responses alone. The protocol requires no time reversal, no ancilla, no double copy, and no weak measurement: a unitary string pulse, forward many-body evolution, and a single local readout suffice. An average over a complete basis of pulse generators yields the OTOC exactly. We further show that the measurement can be made sparse by restricting the pulses to a fixed operator weight, which turns the truncation into an analytically controlled spectral filter: the deterministic bias can be computed before the experiment and cleanly separated from the statistical sampling variance. For Gaussian states, the finite-weight response is exactly proportional to the full OTOC after a known combinatorial rescaling. For quantum chaotic systems, the typical residual is suppressed as a power law in the sector size. Exact-diagonalization benchmarks in the Sachdev-Ye-Kitaev model and a nonintegrable Ising chain confirm these predictions.

### Paper [179]
Title: Stable but Hidden: Identifiability and Experimental Design under Missing Compositional Support
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30070
Authors: Harry Sevi, Joonatan E. M. Laulainen
Abstract: AI-guided scientific discovery often uses inexpensive predictors to allocate scarce verification measurements. We study a structural failure when relevant components have been observed separately but never jointly: distinct admissible response functions can agree on all observed compositions while disagreeing on the missing combinations. Additional same-support data cannot resolve this ambiguity, and agreement among fitted models need not expose it.
  We characterize the unresolved directions as an invisible subspace $\mathcal{N}_T(\mathcal{F}) = \ker \mathcal{R}_T$, whose geometry determines oracle zero-shot ambiguity and minimax risk under a symmetric, bounded ambiguity class. When the missing response lies in an $r$-dimensional, well-conditioned linear basis, optimally designed joint measurements achieve minimax integrated risk $\Theta(\sigma^2 r/m)$, with classical $I$-optimality the fixed-design criterion.
  We validate on materials. In electrocatalysis, a six-dimensional support-derived structural basis (a prior on candidate directions, not an identified physical model) reduces held-out MSE against a dimension-matched generic basis (median paired ratio 0.75, improving 98% of 60 element pairs at $m = 15$), while $I$-optimal and same-basis posterior-variance acquisition show no detected difference. In Materials Project, removing joint property labels reduces $E_{\text{above hull}}$ ranking on 90% of 30 element pairs, more than matched data-removal controls, under a frozen pretrained representation. Pair difficulty is reproducible across learners but poorly predicted by zero-label ensemble disagreement; 20 joint measurements enable transferable pair-level rankings without reliable absolute calibration.
  In practice, experiment selection should ask which relationships the observed support leaves unidentified before asking where a fitted model is least certain.

### Paper [180]
Title: Quantum simulation of low-temperature impurity models
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30187
Authors: Jiaqing Jiang, Nathan Ju
Abstract: Quantum impurity models, which describe a small but strongly-interacting subsystem coupled to a large noninteracting bath, are the computational core of dynamical mean field theory and play an important role in simulating strongly correlated materials. We develop quantum algorithms for efficiently simulating these models at low temperatures and with high precision. For any impurity model on $n$ fermionic modes with fixed impurity size: (1) We give a quantum algorithm that prepares the thermal state at inverse temperature $\beta$ to precision $\delta$ in time $\mathrm{poly}(n,\beta,\log(1/\delta))$. This algorithm is the first to achieve both polynomial scaling in the inverse temperature and polylogarithmic scaling in the precision. When the Hamiltonian has an inverse-polynomial spectral gap, our quantum algorithm prepares its ground state to precision $\delta$ in time $\mathrm{poly}(n,\log(1/\delta))$. (2) We further give a quantum algorithm that prepares a purification of the thermal state in time $\mathrm{poly}(n,\beta,\log(1/\delta))$. Using this purification, we estimate static and dynamical properties with error $\delta$ in time polynomial in $n$ and $\beta$ and linear in $1/\delta$ up to polylogarithmic factors, achieving the Heisenberg scaling. Our algorithms build on quantum Gibbs samplers, which are quantum analogues of Markov chain Monte Carlo methods. By using global jump operators instead of local ones, we avoid the $\exp(\beta)$ overhead commonly associated with quantum Gibbs samplers, making them efficient for impurity models even at low temperatures.

### Paper [181]
Title: Strain-driven terahertz phase modulation in piezoelectric and nonlinear optical crystals: mechanisms, material developments, and modulation strategies
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30194
Authors: Aarifa Muhammed Ashraf, Rajaboopathi Mani
Abstract: Terahertz (THz) technology has witnessed notable developments in sensing, spectroscopy, imaging, and wireless communication which have increased the demand for high-performance THz modulators. Among the various approaches for controlling the THz waves, the application of external stimuli such as optical pumping and thermal or electric fields is widely employed as a promising strategy for modulation. This review focuses on recent advances in THz modulation based on different materials including 2D semiconductors and metamaterials with attention to their response under external stimuli. In addition, strain-driven THz modulation in non-centrosymmetric piezoelectric materials including organic and semi-organic crystals is examined with an emphasis on their ability to establish strong coupling between propagating THz waves and lattice vibrations under piezoelectric resonance conditions. We further highlight earlier reports on strain-driven THz modulation in lithium niobate (LiNbO3) and critically assess the potential of several other nonlinear optical crystals exhibiting piezoelectric resonance peaks suitable for strain-driven THz modulation. An overview of THz modulators integrated with tunable materials is presented highlighting their operating principles and key performance metrics comprising modulation speed, bandwidth, modulation depth and insertion loss.

### Paper [182]
Title: Accelerating interatomic potential development through student-driven exploration and reliability-aware distillation
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30229
Authors: Ruiqi Dong, Haotian Huang, Yubin Hu, Guixiang Zeng
Abstract: Conventional two-stage distillation first establishes a teacher through teacher-driven configuration exploration, a costly first stage for complex materials, and then uses its predictions to supervise students, potentially propagating teacher-label errors. Here, we propose distillation-driven teacher-student co-evolution (DICE), which uses a fast student to accelerate configuration exploration and replaces the fixed teacher-to-student sequence with alternating student and teacher updates. A representation-based support-assessment algorithm routes high-support configurations to teacher labelling and selects low-support configurations for density functional theory (DFT) calculations to update the teacher, thereby reducing the risk of teacher-label error propagation to students. Across Al2O3 (structural ceramics), yttria-stabilised zirconia (solid electrolytes) and MIL-120(Al) (a metal-organic framework), DICE develops reliable compact potentials using only 140-494 target DFT reference configurations per system; total recorded student exploration times per system are just 2.6-5.1% of the corresponding teacher-driven estimates. Combining computational efficiency with broad applicability across diverse material systems, DICE provides a practical route to developing reliable compact interatomic potentials.

### Paper [183]
Title: A response-based self-energy from equation-of-motion coupled-cluster theory and its reduction to GW, GW+SOSEX, and GF2
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30263
Authors: Ajith Perera, Pierre-Fran\c{c}ois Loos
Abstract: We construct a frequency-dependent one-particle correlation self-energy by coupling a reference Green's function to the neutral excitation response obtained from equation-of-motion coupled-cluster theory with singles and doubles (EOM-CCSD). Direct, mixed direct-antisymmetrized, and fully antisymmetrized choices of the external interaction vertices define three approximations, all admitting an explicit spectral representation without numerical frequency integration. In the bare-state limit, the mixed and fully antisymmetrized forms recover second-order Green's-function theory (GF2), whereas the direct form retains only its ring contribution. With a direct random-phase approximation (RPA) response, the direct and mixed forms reduce to $G_0W_0$ and $G_0W_0$ plus second-order screened exchange (SOSEX), respectively. For molecular ionization potentials, replacing the RPA response by EOM-CCSD generally lowers the ionization energies, whereas introducing exchange at one external vertex raises them. These substantial opposing effects partially compensate, although the mixed approximation does not systematically improve upon conventional $G_0W_0$, either against IP-EOM-CCSD or QUESTDB reference energies (aug-cc-pVDZ mean absolute errors relative to the QUESTDB theoretical best estimates: 0.45 vs 0.36~eV). A third-order perturbative analysis reveals that the three approximations retain different contributions and weights of particle-hole diagrams while omitting explicit particle-particle and hole-hole ladder contributions and the static self-energy. In particular, the fully antisymmetrized form recovers GF2 at second order but assigns incorrect weights to its retained third-order diagrams. These findings highlight the importance of a balanced treatment of screening and vertex corrections in constructing accurate self-energy approximations.

### Paper [184]
Title: Dark matter mass fraction in axion stars
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30321
Authors: A. S. Dmitriev, D. G. Levkov, A. G. Panin, I. I. Tkachev
Abstract: QCD axion dark matter is fragmented into small mass miniclusters of high density. With the natural parameters of miniclusters, the time for Bose-Einstein condensation is less than the age of the Universe, which leads to the formation of Bose stars inside these structures. Recently, the problem of the subsequent growth of Bose stars was solved analytically (Dmitriev et al, Phys. Rev. Lett. 132, 091001 (2024)). We use this solution to determine present day mass fraction of dark matter in Bose stars and their mass function.

### Paper [185]
Title: Impact of Topology and Sequence Length on the Pressure-Dependent Collapsibility of Proteins
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30336
Authors: Jiayi Wang, D. Thirumalai, Margaret S. Cheung, Andrei G. Gasic
Abstract: Several physical and evolutionary factors, including temperature, pressure, solvent quality, and natural selection, influence how amino acid sequences adopt their native structures. Although protein folding has been extensively studied, the physical determinants of protein collapse and their relationship to native-state folding remain less understood. In particular, "collapsibility", the intrinsic propensity of a protein sequence to form compact conformations, is distinct from but closely related to "foldability", the ability to reach the native structure. Previous theoretical studies suggested that collapsibility is encoded in native-state topology, potentially reflecting evolutionary selection. However, the roles of solvent-mediated interactions and hydrostatic pressure remain unclear. Here, we extend the theoretical framework of protein collapsibility by incorporating a pressure-dependent desolvation potential to investigate how hydrostatic pressure influences protein collapse. We find that increasing pressure destabilizes the fully folded native state, but can still favor the compact, water-mediated conformations. Furthermore, the magnitude and direction of pressure-induced changes in collapsibility depend strongly on native-state topology, including secondary and tertiary structures. Proteins and domains with a higher proportion of non-local native contacts generally exhibit greater pressure-induced water-mediated collapsibility. These findings highlight the role of native-state topology in determining the pressure sensitivity of protein collapse and provide insights into the interplay among protein structure, solvent-mediated interactions, and environmental perturbations.

### Paper [186]
Title: Liouvillian Quasiparticle Spectroscopy
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30441
Authors: Kai Li, Masahito Ueda
Abstract: Quasiparticles play a central role in understanding closed quantum many-body systems, as they reduce complex many-body problems to effective single-particle descriptions and connect directly to spectroscopic measurements. In open quantum systems, the interplay between coherent dynamics and dissipation gives rise to unique phenomena, such as the Liouvillian skin effect and strong-to-weak spontaneous symmetry breaking. This raises a fundamental question: how can the quasiparticle picture be extended to open quantum systems to account for such phenomena and enable the experimental probing of quasiparticle spectra? Here we introduce coherent and incoherent Liouvillian quasiparticles, defined by responses to perturbations acting on one side and both sides of the density matrix, respectively. We propose spectroscopy protocols that couple the system to auxiliary levels, either coherently or via a dissipative pulse, to measure the corresponding complex quasiparticle energies. Numerical simulations of Liouvillians with skin effects validate these protocols, revealing nontrivial spectral winding associated with unidirectional transport in real space. Notably, coherent and incoherent quasiparticles can propagate in opposite directions within the same system. Furthermore, in models exhibiting weak and strong-to-weak $U(1)$ spontaneous symmetry breaking, our results unveil gapless Nambu-Goldstone modes in the coherent and incoherent quasiparticle spectra, respectively; the other quasiparticle spectrum remains gapped in each case. Our work provides a unified framework for theoretically describing and experimentally probing coherent and incoherent quasiparticles in open quantum many-body systems.

### Paper [187]
Title: Universal stabilizer correlations in chaotic eigenstates
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30485
Authors: Zhi-Han Liu, Hao-Yue Qi, Wei Zheng
Abstract: Entanglement and nonstabilizerness provide complementary perspectives on quantum-state structure. In this work, we find that the stabilizer correlation, defined as the difference between the R\'enyi-$2$ mutual information and the mutual stabilizer R\'enyi entropy, approaches a universal constant for chaotic eigenstates. This constant is determined solely by the symmetries, and quantifies the correlation that does not contribute to nonstabilizerness. We identify this universal stabilizer correlation analytically via random-state ensembles, which give $\log_2 7$ (GOE) and $\log_2 4$(GUE) without extra symmetries, $\log_2 14$ and $\log_2 8$ under spin-inversion parity or half-filling magnetization. While imposing reflection or translation symmetry leaves these values unchanged. Exact calculations for a chaotic mixed-field Ising chain confirm the predicted plateau and finite-size approach. Integrable free-fermion and strongly disordered eigenstates behave qualitatively differently. This universal stabilizer correlation reveals a characteristic structure of chaotic eigenstates beyond entanglement or nonstabilizerness alone.

### Paper [188]
Title: 1.2 dB Insertion Loss in a Monolithically Integrated Silicon Mach-Zehnder Optical Isolator Using Stoichiometric Ce:YIG
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30501
Authors: Zhenyuan Ren, Di Wu, Tianchi Zhang, Yizhi Chen, Junxian Wang, Zixuan Wei, Jun Qin, Lei Bi
Abstract: Integrated optical isolators play a crucial role in photonic integrated circuits (PICs) by effectively stabilizing optical signals, making them indispensable components for advanced photonic systems. However, integrated magneto-optical (MO) isolators suffer from their high insertion loss, which is mainly caused by the relatively low FoM of Ce:YIG MO thin films deposited on silicon. Here, we show that film stoichiometry plays a key role in controlling the FoM of Ce:YIG. We observed garnet phase Fe-rich and Ce-rich nano-clusters in non-stoichiometric Ce:YIG. By using an iron-deficient target, we demonstrated that nearly stoichiometric Ce:YIG films on Si(100) could be fabricated, eliminating the nano-cluster defects. Consequently, the film exhibited a saturation Faraday rotation angle of -5523 deg/cm and a figure of merit of 72.7 deg/dB, which is 1.7 times higher compared to nonstoichiometric films. An MZI-type optical isolator on SiN fabricated by this method demonstrated a record low insertion loss of 1.2 dB and isolation ratio of 27.6 dB at 1556 nm wavelength. Our results provide an important step toward the practical application of integrated MO nonreciprocal photonic devices.

### Paper [189]
Title: Density of states for random Schr\"odinger operators with linearly correlated heavy-tailed disorder
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30529
Authors: Margherita Disertori, Luca Fresta, Liza Schonlau
Abstract: We study the density of states of random Schr\"odinger operators with linearly correlated heavy-tailed disorder. Using an integration-by-parts supersymmetric representation, we prove smoothness of the density of states and Wegner estimates in the strong-disorder regime. We are particularly interested in mixed-sign correlation kernels, which serve as a toy model for the hyperbolic structures arising in the analysis of averaged squared Green's functions. Our approach applies to a broad class of correlation kernels and disorder distributions, including $\alpha$-stable laws.

### Paper [190]
Title: Geometry of ground states in the long-range Ising model
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30604
Authors: Serena Dipierro, Riccardo Sabbatini, Enrico Valdinoci
Abstract: We study the ground states of the ferromagnetic Ising model on $\mathbb{Z}^d$ with long-range interactions and prescribed exterior data. We first show that the half-space configuration is a ground state, by means of a discrete calibration (which is the lattice counterpart of the calibrations of Cabr\'e and Pagliari for nonlocal minimal surfaces). In dimension $d\ge2$ and for positive kernels, the half-space configuration is the unique minimizer in every finite domain, and it is "stable" with respect to perturbations of the exterior datum whose energy is below half of a positive excitation gap. This rigidity has no counterpart in the continuum.
  For general exterior data and positive kernels, we show that along a monotone family the minimizer is unique except for finitely many data. This can be considered a "generic uniqueness result" that provides the discrete counterpart of a recent work of Dipierro, Fern\'andez-Real and Valdinoci in the continuum.
  We then introduce a pointwise notion, called perception, which is the lattice counterpart of the instability and stickiness phenomena for nonlocal minimal surfaces discovered by Dipierro, Savin and Valdinoci. We give a local criterion for the half-space configuration to be perceptive at a given site.
  Finally, we consider a box with exterior datum $+1$ above and below a slab and $-1$ on its sides. For fractional kernels with small exponent, the minimizer is $+1$ in the whole box, and its positive phase is connected. For short-range kernels, instead, the negative phase may enter the box, and the positive phase may be disconnected. Moreover, if the datum is $-1$ around the box and $+1$ only at distance two from it, the configuration identically $-1$ is perceptive at every boundary site of the box for fractional kernels with small exponent.

### Paper [191]
Title: Noise-Immune Attractors in Stochastically Driven Non-Hermitian Systems
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30811
Authors: Hanieh Najafzadeh, Radmehr Jafari, Abdollah Langari
Abstract: Temporal stochastic fluctuations generically destroy quantum coherence and accelerate defect generation in driven quantum systems. Here, we demonstrate that non-Hermitian dynamics can fundamentally reverse this trend by stabilizing noise-resilient late-time states. By developing an exact noise master equation for non-Hermitian systems subject to Gaussian fluctuations, we uncover a projective noise-invariance mechanism governed by T-symmetry-broken Liouvillian attractors. This mechanism produces robust defect-density plateaus and yields an exact geometric classification into noise-independent, fully polarized, and continuously tunable regimes. We substantiate these results in an imbalanced-pairing non-Hermitian Kitaev model, a prototypical pseudo-Hermitian system, and propose their experimental observation in coupled photonic waveguides. Our findings identify non-Hermitian attractor dynamics as a versatile route to controlling noise-induced excitations beyond conventional anti-Kibble-Zurek scaling.

### Paper [192]
Title: Exponential advantage in quantum observation with one extra qubit
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30876
Authors: Zhenyu Du, Xiongfeng Ma, Vlatko Vedral, Jinzhao Sun
Abstract: While quantum technologies promise to revolutionise how we observe the physical world, achieving substantial advantages with minimal overhead remains a central pursuit. Here, we show that augmenting a quantum machine with just one extra qubit yields an exponential advantage in probing quantum many-body dynamics. We propose an algorithm that uses this qubit to coherently interfere dynamical trajectories, simultaneously estimating exponentially many multi-time correlators with exponentially fewer evolutions than any ancilla-free scheme. Remarkably, this advantage holds even when the ancilla-free machine has unrestricted coherence time and arbitrary adaptive control, and persists even for a single correlator. We further prove that extending coherence time exponentially enhances observational power, establishing our protocol's optimality in both space and time. Finally, we numerically demonstrate our approach by predicting higher-order probe signals in a many-body system with orders-of-magnitude fewer experimental runs than previous methods. These results highlight the broad utility of our framework across quantum learning, sensing, and simulation.

### Paper [193]
Title: Gradient formula from the sphere for perturbative RG flows near critical dimensions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31050
Authors: Marten Reehorst
Abstract: We study the gradient property of perturbative renormalisation group flows in $d=d_c-\varepsilon$, with a potential and a metric regular at $\varepsilon=0$ up to logarithms of gauge couplings. We state properties of the renormalised functional with local couplings on the sphere $S^d$: finiteness, a local Weyl anomaly, the Ward identity of a conformal Killing vector, rotation properties and statements on the free theory. From them we derive a Ward identity without anomaly for a combination of the functional and its anomaly. Its first variation is a gradient formula whose symmetric metric contains the two-point function on the sphere and whose potential at fixed points is, in an extended form of the properties, proportional to the sphere free energy $\tilde F$ up to a constant. Sources of relevant operators enter through their Weyl variation, and in a scheme which we construct the gradient formula persists, with a nondegenerate metric if a matrix of the free theory is invertible. For multiscalar $\phi^4$ theory and for scalars and Dirac fermions with Yukawa and quartic couplings in $d=4-\varepsilon$, for sextic scalars in $d=3-\varepsilon$ and for cubic scalars in $d=6-\varepsilon$, we derive all these properties from a finiteness hypothesis. The gradient formula then holds to all orders for the beta function defined on the sphere. For interacting gauge theories the properties remain assumptions.

### Paper [194]
Title: Direct electron cooling at millikelvin temperatures with quantum-well heat pump
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2207.14237
Authors: Chulin Wang, Thomas Douglas, Matthew A Grayson
Abstract: Unconventional solid-state cooling is proposed whereby the electron subband degeneracy of a semiconductor quantum well is cycled at cryogenic temperatures. By "expanding" electrons to higher subband degeneracy and "compressing" to lower degeneracy in an Otto cycle, a stack of ultra-wide 150 nm GaAs quantum wells filling a 1 cm3 volume could be operated as a heat pump to pump 26 uW at 100 mK. Operated as a refrigerator, it can piggy-back onto dilution refrigerators to cool from T_base to T_base/4. Possible cooling applications include quantum computers and infrared detectors.

### Paper [195]
Title: High-temperature superconductivity from kinetic energy
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.07292
Authors: Hanbit Oh, Hui Yang, Ya-Hui Zhang
Abstract: Superconductivity is usually assumed to arise from attractive interaction. In this work we show that strong pairing is possible soley from kinetic energy even without a net attraction. We demonstrate a high-temperature kinetic superconductor in a simple lattice model with nearest-neighbor hopping ($t$) projected onto a constrained Hilbert space, analogous to the $t$-$J$ model with $J=0$, where kinetic magnetism has been previously studied. Using density matrix renormalization group (DMRG) on cylinders up to width $L_y=8$, we find a superconducting ground state exhibiting a key difference from high-$T_c$ cuprates: both the pairing gap and phase stiffness \textit{increase} with doping ($x$). We find pairing gaps, determined from spin and single-electron charge gaps, exceeding $1.5t$. This model can be realized within the double Kondo lattice model, relevant to bilayer nickelates, in the limit of strong inter-layer spin coupling ($J_\perp/t \rightarrow +\infty$) and a balancing inter-layer repulsion ($V$). Importantly, the double Kondo model does not fundamentally restrict $J_\perp/t$, suggesting the potential for high critical temperatures ($T_c$) approaching $0.5t$. While this idealized limit predicts large pairing gaps, we show a smooth connection to the more realistic regime with $J_\perp \sim t$, albeit with a reduced pairing gap of approximately $0.1t$. Assuming $t \sim 10^3$ K in typical solid state systems, our model suggests the exciting possibility of achieving $T_c$ of hundreds of Kelvin. We propose searching for bilayer materials with reduced out-of-plane lattice constants to better approximate the conditions of our ideal model.

### Paper [196]
Title: P-adic numbers and kernels
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.15377
Authors: Simone Franchini
Abstract: We discuss the relation between p-adic numbers and kernels in view of a recent large deviation theory for mean-field spin glasses. As an application we show several fundamental properties of numerical bases in kernel language. In particular, we show that the Derrida's Generalized Random Energy Model can be interpreted as a (random) numerical base. We also show an application to the Primon gas and the Riemann Zeta Function by constructing a kernel representation of the Primon gas based on a finite p-base, thereby establishing a concrete link between number theory and kernel theory.

### Paper [197]
Title: Blanket representation for spin networks with n-body interactions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21101
Authors: Simone Franchini
Abstract: This paper extends the Blanket representation of [Universal scaling limits for spin networks via martingale methods, Franchini, S., Proc. R. Soc. A, 481 (2025)] from systems with two-body interactions to multi-spin (or n-body) interactions. This generalization allows for the exploration of broader physical phenomena where higher order interactions are significant.

### Paper [198]
Title: Strange correlator and string order parameter for non-invertible symmetry protected topological phases in 1+1d
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00673
Authors: Da-Chuan Lu, Fu Xu, Yi-Zhuang You
Abstract: We construct strange correlators and string order parameters for non-invertible symmetry protected topological phases (NISPTs) in 1+1d spin chains. These diagnostics and the entanglement spectrum are connected to the interface algebra between two NISPTs. To compare phases, we first use finite-depth circuits of locally symmetric gates to minimize the bond dimension of each matrix product state. The resulting action tensors determine local strange charged operators and the charge decorations of truncated symmetry operators. The strange correlator probes the representation of the interface algebra realized by the two states. Higher-dimensional irreducible components yield nontrivial strange charged operators. For the distinct phases studied here, we identify such operators with long-range strange correlations. The full interface algebra admits a one-dimensional representation precisely when the phases coincide. For an on-site symmetry with a symmetric product reference state, the dimensions of the irreducible representations of the interface algebra also constrain degeneracies in the entanglement spectrum. We use unitary Drinfeld twists of a finite-dimensional Hopf $C^*$-algebra $H$ to construct general fixed point $\mathrm{Rep}(H)$ SPTs, action tensors and string order parameters. Our examples include the three $\mathrm{Rep}(D_8)$ NISPTs and intrinsically mixed $G\times\mathrm{Rep}(G)$ SPTs with explicit Hamiltonians for $G=S_3$. For the self-dual Hopf algebra $A_{12}$, we construct Hamiltonians and string order parameters for the two $\mathrm{Rep}(A_{12})$ SPT phases and evaluate a strange correlator between them. We also outline a geometric extension to higher dimensions.

### Paper [199]
Title: Interaction-enhanced quantum to classical transport crossover temperature in a Luttinger liquid
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05356
Authors: Yen-Wen Lu, Michael Mulligan
Abstract: Strange metals are highly entangled gapless states of matter that exhibit anomalous transport, such as linear in temperature resistivity, over more than a decade of temperature. Why a single power law should be so robust is an open question. We propose a scenario in which interactions enhance the domain of certain scattering regimes, effectively suppressing other ``would-be regimes." We test this proposal in a one-dimensional Luttinger liquid coupled to a one-dimensional acoustic phonon. We use the memory matrix formalism to calculate the dc electrical and thermal conductivities at low and high temperatures, relative to the Debye cutoff on phonon frequencies, in both the ``clean" (umklapp scattering) and ``dirty" (disorder scattering) limits. We find the crossover temperature separating the low and high temperature regimes to be interaction-dependent, with repulsive interactions substantially increasing it, generally by more than an order of magnitude. This provides a concrete illustration for how interactions can extend a single transport regime over a wider temperature range.

### Paper [200]
Title: Intrinsic disorder in the candidate quantum spin ice Pr$_2$Zr$_2$O$_7$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.10101
Authors: T. J. Hicken, P. Meadows, D. Prabhakaran, A. Szab\'o, S. E. Dutton, C. Castelnovo, K. Moovendaran, T. S. Northam de la Fuente, L. Mangin-Thro, G. B. G. Stenning, M. J. Gutmann, G. Sala, M. B. Stone, P. F. Henry, S. A. Barnett, D. R. Allan, D. J. Voneshen, J. P. Goff
Abstract: Pr$_2$Zr$_2$O$_7$ is a promising example of a quantum spin liquid, where structural disorder plays a key role in enhancing quantum mechanical effects. Here we have determined the intrinsic defect structure of Pr$_2$Zr$_2$O$_7$ using neutron and x-ray scattering techniques supported by density functional theory. Our results explain the crystal field excitations by considering the non-magnetic singlets that arise on neighbouring sites. This makes a significant contribution towards the observed broadening of pinch points in the magnetic diffuse scattering, which was previously attributed purely to quantum effects.

### Paper [201]
Title: Probing Fractional Quantum Hall states in weakly interacting Fermi gases
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.22606
Authors: Viktor Bekassy, Mikael Fogelstr\"om, Johannes Hofmann
Abstract: Quantum gases are used to simulate the physics of the lowest Landau level with neutral atoms, which in the simplest setup is achieved by rotating the gas at the confining harmonic trap frequency, a requirement that is difficult to meet in practice. However, it is known that lowest Landau level states appear among the excited states of weakly interacting gases, even in a nonrotating trap, by working at fixed finite angular momentum. Thus, even in the absence of rotations or for moderate rotation frequencies, fractional quantum Hall (FQH) states can be accessed as excited states at finite angular momentum. Motivated by this, and by recent experimentally demonstrated excitation protocols for two particles, we show that such states can be selectively addressed at larger particle numbers, and we propose imaging techniques to resolve their distinct microscopic structure. In particular, we suggest that recently developed excitation and imaging techniques for rotating few-fermion systems allow for a detailed experimental investigation of FQH wave functions and of the crossover to large particle numbers. We illustrate the evolution of the excitation spectrum with rotations at weak interactions for $N=6$ spin-balanced fermions and show how sampling of the few-body wave function allows one to discriminate between total guiding-center, center-of-mass vortex, and hyperradial excitations, thus providing detailed and versatile probes of the microscopic structure of FQH states.

### Paper [202]
Title: Classification of Thouless pumps with non-invertible symmetries and implications for Floquet phases
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01626
Authors: Yabo Li, Matteo Dell'Acqua, Aditi Mitra
Abstract: We study symmetry preserving adiabatic and Floquet dynamics of one-dimensional systems. Using quasiadiabatic evolution, we establish a correspondence between adiabatic cycles and invertible defects generated by spatially truncated Thouless pump operators. Employing the classification of gapped phases by module categories, we show that the Thouless pumps are classified by the group of autoequivalences of the module category. We then explicitly construct Thouless pump operators for minimal lattice models with $\text{Vec}_G$, Rep($G$), and Rep($H$) symmetries, and show how the Thouless pump operators have the group structure of autoequivalences. The Thouless pump operators, together with Hamiltonians with gapped ground states, are then used to construct Floquet drives. An analytic solution for the Floquet phase diagram characterized by winding numbers is constructed when the Floquet drives obey an Onsager algebra. Our approach points the way to a general connection between distinct Thouless pumps and distinct families of Floquet phases.

### Paper [203]
Title: Transport of Dirac magnons driven by gauge fields
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.13362
Authors: Luis Fern\'andez, Ka Shen, Leandro O. Nascimento, Van S\'ergio Alves, Roberto E. Troncoso, Nicolas Vidal-Silva
Abstract: We present a unified quantum field theory for Dirac magnons coupled to emergent gauge fields. At zero temperature, any space- and time-dependent gauge perturbation drives magnons out of equilibrium, generating spin currents and magnon accumulation without conventional thermal or chemical potential gradients. For a honeycomb ferromagnet, we derive closed-form expressions for the induced density and current. In the DC limit, the transverse spin conductivity quantizes to $\sigma^{xy}=\alpha^2\text{sgn}(m)\hbar/4\pi$, a magnonic analog of the quantum Hall effect, where $m$ is the topological magnon mass and $\alpha$ a dimensionless coupling constant. In the AC regime, the conductivity exhibits a sharp resonance when the drive frequency matches the topological gap $\Delta$, signaling interband transitions. Our work establishes gauge fields as a versatile tool for controlling magnon transport and reveals topologically protected quantized responses.

### Paper [204]
Title: Pair-density-wave superconductivity and Anderson's theorem in bilayer nickelates
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15023
Authors: Hanbit Oh, Ya-Hui Zhang
Abstract: The recent experimental observations of high temperature superconductivity in bilayer nickelate have attracted lots of attentions. Previous studies have assumed a mirror symmetry $\mathcal M$ between the two layers and focused on uniform and clean superconducting states. Here, we show that breaking this mirror symmetry via an applied displacement field can stabilize a pair-density-wave (PDW) superconductor, which is similar to the Fulde--Ferrell--Larkin--Ovchinnikov (FFLO) state, but at zero magnetic field. Based on a mean-field analysis of a model of $d_{x^2-y^2}$ orbital with an effective inter-layer attraction, we demonstrate that the PDW phase is robust over a wide range of displacement field, interlayer hopping strengths, and electron fillings. Finally, we analyze disorder effects on interlayer superconductivity within the first Born approximation. Based on symmetry considerations, we show that pairing is weaken by disorders which break the mirror symmetry, even with unbroken time reversal symmetry. Our results establish bilayer nickelate as a tunable platform for realizing finite-momentum pairing and for exploring generalized disorder effects.

### Paper [205]
Title: Universal electrical transport of composite Fermi liquid to Metal transition in Moir\'e systems
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.15334
Authors: Youxuan Wang, Rongning Liu, Feng Liu, Xueyang Song
Abstract: We study universal electrical transport at continuous transitions between a composite Fermi liquid and a Fermi liquid in moir\'e Chern bands at fillings $\nu=-1/2$ and $-3/4$. The critical theory couples Dirac fermions at a boson Laughlin--superfluid transition to a spinon Fermi surface through Chern--Simons gauge fields. We derive the Ioffe--Larkin rule for the full resistivity tensor and solve a quantum Boltzmann equation at leading order in a large-$N$ expansion. Spinon Landau damping adds a quasistatic scattering channel with weight independent of the spinon Fermi energy. At $N=1$ we obtain clean-limit resistivity jumps $\Delta\rho_{xx}\approx5.896\,h/e^2$ and $0.1274\,h/e^2$ at the two fillings.

### Paper [206]
Title: Mixed-state Phases from Higher-order SSPTs with Kramers-Wannier Symmetry
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.03455
Authors: Aswin Parayil Mana, Zijian Song, Fei Yan, Tzu-Chieh Wei
Abstract: Mixed-state phases have recently attracted significant attention as a generalization beyond their pure-state counterparts. Prominent examples include mixed-state symmetry-protected topological (mSPT) phases and the strong-to-weak symmetry breaking (SWSSB) phases. It has been shown recently that mSPT phases admit a holographic dual description in terms of higher-order subsystem SPT phases. In this work, we investigate the mixed-state phases obtained by tracing out the bulk degrees of freedom of higher-order subsystem SPT phases protected by non-invertible symmetries. We find that the resulting mixed-states exhibit the coexistence of the symmetry-protected topological order and SWSSB. We also use the interface as a probe to characterize the mixed-state phases, and specifically, when there is no local modification to preserve the symmetries across the interface, the two sides of the interface are in distinct phases.

### Paper [207]
Title: What is a minimum work transition in stochastic thermodynamics?
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.11995
Authors: Paolo Muratore-Ginanneschi, Julia Sanders
Abstract: We reassess the concept of transition at minimum work in classical stochastic finite-time thermodynamics, when the system dynamics is modelled by a diffusion process. We show that a well-posed formulation of the optimal control problem corresponding to the minimization of the mean work done on the system during a finite-time transition necessarily requires taking into account speed limits on control protocols. This fact has major qualitative consequences. First, it permits to discriminate between optimal swift engineered equilibration and transitions at minimum work. Second, it shows that in the limit when speed limits are removed, only transitions specified by generalized Schr\"odinger bridges admit a consistent physical interpretation. To illustrate these points, we focus on the simplest model problem: a levitating particle in a Gaussian moving trap.

### Paper [208]
Title: Origin of Reduced Coercive Field in ScAlN: Synergy of Structural Softening and Dynamic Atomic Correlations
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18710
Authors: Ryotaro Sahashi, Po-Yen Chen, Teruyasu Mizoguchi
Abstract: Among wurtzite-type ferroelectrics, scandium-doped aluminum nitride (ScAlN) has emerged as a leading candidate for CMOS-compatible low-voltage memory, combining strong spontaneous polarization with process compatibility. A remarkable feature of this system is the pronounced reduction of the coercive field ($E_c$) with increasing Sc concentration; however, its microscopic origin remains poorly understood at the atomic scale, particularly under finite temperature and applied electric fields. Here, we employ an atomistic simulation framework that integrates a density-functional-theory-accurate machine-learning force field with an equivariant neural-network-based Born effective charge model to perform large-scale electric-field-induced molecular dynamics simulations at near-first-principles accuracy. The framework correctly reproduces key experimentally observed trends, including the decrease in the c/a ratio and the monotonic reduction of $E_c$ with increasing Sc content. Beyond static structural softening, we uncover a dynamic mechanism underlying $E_c$ reduction. Sc atoms exhibit larger thermal vibrations and begin to displace earlier during switching, acting as dynamic triggers for polarization reversal. Moreover, the displacement correlation between Sc and Al atoms evolves systematically with composition, enhancing cooperative atomic rearrangements and lowering the effective switching barrier. These results demonstrate that $E_c$ reduction in ScAlN arises from the synergy of structural softening and dynamic correlation evolution, providing a new perspective for designing hexagonal ferroelectrics.

### Paper [209]
Title: Origin of the tetragonal-to-hexagonal phase transitions in Fe-doped BaTiO$_3$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.22571
Authors: Zhiyuan Li, Ruiwen Xie, Hongbin Zhang
Abstract: Based on detailed first-principles calculations, we investigate the tetragonal-to-hexagonal phase transition in Fe-doped BaTiO$_3$. Free energy calculations confirm a crossover from the tetragonal to hexagonal phases at 2.7--6\% Fe on cooling from the sintering temperature, in agreement with experimental observations, where comparative calculations show that neither CaTiO$_3$ nor SrTiO$_3$ exhibits similar behavior under equivalent substitution. Furthermore, three possible mechanisms are quantified: oxygen vacancies shift the crossover concentration from 2.7--6\% to 1.1--4.7\%, Jahn-Teller distortions are confined to the tetragonal phase, both favoring tetragonal-to-hexagonal phase transitions; whereas the tolerance factor is reduced in comparison with that of pristine BaTiO$_3$ for reasonable Fe valence states, disfavoring the occurrence of the hexagonal phases. Detailed analysis on the electronic structure reveals that the charge redistribution induced by oxygen vacancy is strongly orbital dependent, with the added charge occupying the $d_{z^2}$ orbital.

### Paper [210]
Title: Phase separation by polar active transport
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01057
Authors: Sudipta Pattanayak, Alfredo Sciortino, Laurent Blanchoin, Manuel Th\'ery, Jean-Francois Joanny
Abstract: We propose an active Cahn-Hilliard theory for the dynamics of a new type of phase transition where the driving force is not the direct interactions between the two separating components, but their active sorting by a third polar species. This third species can transport the other two along its polarity in opposite directions, thus separating them. Inspired by recent experiments where molecular motors that walk in opposite directions along microtubules are sorted into separated domains, our theoretical description of this process introduces a new mechanism for active phase separation and could serve as a model for the organization of biological material in space inside cells. We predict the formation of motor domains, and further show that they can either coarsen to form macroscopic phases or reach a finite micro- or mesoscopic steady state size, these latter due to an arrest of coarsening through activity.

### Paper [211]
Title: Magnetism and symmetry of superconducting gap in LaFeAsO from dynamical mean-field theory
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.18582
Authors: S. L. Skornyakov, V. I. Anisimov, A. A. Katanin
Abstract: By employing a combined method of density functional theory and dynamical mean field theory (DFT+DMFT) we investigate the effect of electronic correlations on the magnetic and superconducting properties of the iron-based parent compound LaFeAsO. We find that the static non-local susceptibility $\chi({\bf q})$ and the dynamical spin structure factor $S({\mathbf q},\omega)$ exhibit a peak at the in-plane wave vector ${\mathbf Q}=(\pi,\pi)$, which is strongly enhanced upon inclusion of dynamical vertex corrections in the ladder approximation, leading to magnetic instability. Considering the eigenfunctions of the Bethe-Salpeter equation with the vertex, obtained within the second order perturbation theory, as well as the ladder approach containing dynamic interaction vertices, in agreement with earlier weak-coupling-based studies of LaFeAsO, we obtain a close competition between $d$-wave and $s_{\pm}$ order parameters, dominating in the second-order and ladder approach, respectively. We argue that the dominating $s_{\pm}$ instability in the ladder DFT+DMFT approach is related to the reduced degree of magnetic frustration by itinerant degrees of freedom due to only partially formed local magnetic moments. Our study shows that dynamic correlation effects do not change the type of the leading superconducting instability in LaFeAsO.

### Paper [212]
Title: Coordination-number dependent universality in Mixed Wet Percolation
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.18915
Authors: Jnana Ranjan Das, Santanu Sinha, Alex Hansen, Sitangshu Bikas Santra
Abstract: Mixed-wet percolation was introduced recently in the context of two-phase flow in porous media. In this model, the sites of the primal lattice are occupied with a certain probability $p$, and bonds are placed on the dual lattice between two adjacent occupied and unoccupied sites of the primal lattice. The occupied bonds on the dual lattice form perimeter bond clusters. We investigate here the scaling properties of the geometric quantities associated with the perimeter bond clusters of mixed-wet percolation on the dual triangular and dual honeycomb lattices in two dimensions. The perimeter bond clusters on the triangular lattice represent the boundaries of the site clusters on the primal lattice, whereas the perimeter bond clusters on the honeycomb lattice represent their hulls. Because of the low coordination number $z$ of the honeycomb lattice, the external and internal perimeter bond clusters remain isolated. Although mixed-wet percolation on the dual triangular lattice with a higher coordination number ($z=6$) exhibits ordinary cluster percolation, the model on the dual honeycomb lattice with a lower coordination number ($z=3$) exhibits hull percolation. Such a $z$ dependent crossover of universality in mixed-wet percolation is rare in the percolation literature. However, the external and internal perimeter bond clusters associated with a site cluster combine to form cluster envelopes. These cluster envelopes belong to the cluster percolation universality class.

### Paper [213]
Title: Characterizing electronic scattering rates with transport in multiterminal devices
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.03030
Authors: Jack H. Farrell, Andrew Lucas
Abstract: Strongly interacting electrons in clean two-dimensional devices are theorized to exhibit many distinct transport regimes, such as ballistic, hydrodynamic, or diffusive. Realistic samples often lie in crossover regimes between these idealized limits. We show how a single experiment on a multiterminal device can distinguish these regimes and constrain the relevant scattering rates without space-resolved imaging. Using a linearized Boltzmann model in a five-terminal geometry, we find that current partition among the drain contacts diagnoses the ballistic-hydrodynamic-Ohmic crossover and allows extraction of momentum-relaxing and momentum-conserving scattering rates in the crossover regime.
