We show that the resulting TEM modes are governed by an advection-diffusion-type equation for the transverse electrostatic potential, establishing a direct mathematical connection between axion electrodynamics and advection-diffusion phenomena. We illustrate this connection through explicit solutions and field profiles for cylindrical waveguides, considering uniform and rigid rotational drift fields, both individually and in combination, as well as different values of the parameter controlling the advection-to-diffusion ratio (P\'eclet number). These results establish a transport-based framework for understanding how spatial variations of the axion-electrodynamics background shape the transverse electromagnetic fields in hollow-conductor waveguides.

### Paper [267]
Title: Exact Fractional Chern Insulators from Hubbard Interactions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25010
Authors: Jonah Herzog-Arbeitman, Rui-Heng Liu, Jiangping Hu, Chen Fang
Abstract: Laughlin's wavefunction and its parent interaction, Haldane's pseudopotential, underpin our analytic understanding of fractional quantum Hall effect. They are an exact pair in the sense that the wavefunction is the unique, zero-energy ground state of its frustration-free parent Hamiltonian. In this work, we propose exact pairs of many-body wavefunctions for fractional Chern insulators in tight binding models with onsite Hubbard interactions as their parents. The key to our construction is introduction of a Landau-dual Chern band, a multi-orbital Chern band obtained from directly sampling the spin-$s$ Dirac Landau level on a Bravais lattice. The exact many-body wavefunctions are given, analytically, in terms of the matrix coordinate $X+iY+J$, where $(X,Y)$ are the lattice coordinates and $J$ is a nilpotent matrix in spin space. The constructed exact pairs cover the fractional Chern states of the Laughlin, the Read-Rezayi, and the Halperin series. Our results prove that FCI ground states exist beyond ideal bands.

### Paper [268]
Title: The phase diagram of fluid hydrogen near melting from machine-learned interatomic potentials trained on quantum Monte Carlo
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25178
Authors: Shubhang Goswami, Hongwei Niu, Ilnur Saitov, Mathieu Istas, Yubo Yang, Scott Jensen, Markus Holzmann, Carlo Pierleoni, David Ceperley
Abstract: Dissociation of molecular hydrogen increasing temperature or pressure may either occur gradually or abruptly, via a phase transition. Despite considerable experimental and theoretical efforts, the phase diagram of dense hydrogen at temperatures above solid melting is not precisely known. Here, we perform classical and quantum Path Integral molecular dynamics simulations of dense hydrogen using two machine-learned interatomic potentials both trained on quantum Monte Carlo energies and forces. The resulting phase diagram is constructed using state-of-the-art techniques. Both models show a first-order phase transition from the molecular to the atomic liquid terminating in critical points whose locations were determined using finite-size scaling techniques. Triple points, located at the highest temperature of the melting curve, are found at the intersection of the liquid-liquid lines with the melting curve and should lead to clear signatures in ramp compression experiments.

### Paper [269]
Title: momipy: A time-resolved momentum microscopy data analysis workflow
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25888
Authors: Paul Werner (I. Physikalisches Institut, Georg-August-Universit\"at G\"ottingen, Germany), Wiebke Bennecke (I. Physikalisches Institut, Georg-August-Universit\"at G\"ottingen, Germany), Jan Philipp Bange (I. Physikalisches Institut, Georg-August-Universit\"at G\"ottingen, Germany), Junde Liu (I. Physikalisches Institut, Georg-August-Universit\"at G\"ottingen, Germany), Daniel Steil (I. Physikalisches Institut, Georg-August-Universit\"at G\"ottingen, Germany), Marcel Reutzel (Fachbereich Physik, Philipps-Universit\"at Marburg, Germany, mar.quest | Marburg Center for Quantum Materials and Sustainable Technologies, Marburg, Germany), G. S. Matthijs Jansen (I. Physikalisches Institut, Georg-August-Universit\"at G\"ottingen, Germany, Leiden Institute of Physics, Leiden University, Leiden, Netherlands), Stefan Mathias (I. Physikalisches Institut, Georg-August-Universit\"at G\"ottingen, Germany, International Center for Advanced Studies of Energy Conversion)
Abstract: Photoemission momentum microscopy is an emerging approach to photoelectron spectroscopy that is transforming electronic band structure mapping at both laboratory-based and large-scale facilities. Its extension to time-resolved measurements has recently enabled major advances, ranging from the femtosecond imaging of exciton wavefunctions to dark-field photoelectron microscopy of light-induced dynamics and strong light-matter interactions. However, the rapid acquisition of high-dimensional data sets (including electron in-plane momenta $k_x$ and $k_y$, kinetic energy $E_\mathrm{kin}$, time $\Delta t$, and more) poses substantial challenges for data handling and analysis. A typical workflow encompasses data acquisition, exploratory visualization, calibration and targeted feature extraction, with data management ideally guided by FAIR principles. Here, we present momipy, a Python package that streamlines the momentum microscopy data analysis workflow efficiently and at low computational cost. Among other features, momipy provides interactive 4D data visualization, energy and momentum calibration, space-charge correction, and a broad selection of multidimensional data analysis routines, including binning, momentum-map extraction and time-, energy- and momentum-resolved model fitting. momipy is accompanied by a selection of interactive example scripts, supporting adoption of momipy by both experienced researchers and users new to momentum microscopy.

### Paper [270]
Title: Nonstandard quantum statistics for strongly correlated systems: Superexclusion in momentum space and comparison with exact results
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26489
Authors: Piotr Kuterba, Mateusz W\'ojcik, J\'ozef Spa{\l}ek
Abstract: We analyze in detail nonstandard statistics for strongly correlated fermions, which comprise the statistical spin liquid (SSL) and model non-Fermi liquid (NFL) cases. Both of those cases are based on the total exclusion of local doubly occupancies ($the~superexclusion$) either in real space (SSL case) or in reciprocal ($\mathbf{k}$) space (NFL). The results obtained on a purely physical ground are supplemented with the exact analysis of those coming from microscopic Hubbard-like model in $\mathbf{k}$ space. Specifically, we discuss the transmutation of those statistics to that of localized magnetic moments, as well as the transformation of the Fermi liquid into a strongly correlated fermionic liquid or Mott insulating state; the latter taking place for the half-filled-band situation. The role of exclusion of double occupancies in either real or reciprocal space in the appearance of the correlated phase is stressed. Conclusions concerning the universal features of the models, independence of either the real- or $\mathbf{k}$-space representation, are also discussed. One of those is the boson to fermion statistics transmutation which in the limit of strong repulsive interboson interaction leads to the particle localization instead to the Bose-Einstein condensation. The superexclusion statistics represents the infinite-interaction limit of the $\mathbf{k}$-Hubbard model.

### Paper [271]
Title: Pushy Random Walk on the Bethe lattice: a Galton-Watson perspective
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.27386
Authors: J\'er\'emie Klinger
Abstract: Pushy random walks are minimal models of transport in deformable media, in which the walker displaces the obstacles it bumps into rather than being blocked by them. On the Bethe lattice, we show that the walkable structure dynamically created by a pushy walker can be generated a priori as an explicit multi-type Galton-Watson tree, so that the walker reduces to a simple random walk on a static random tree, and its escape to infinity coincides with the survival of the underlying branching process. Standard generating-function tools then give direct access to the escape properties of these walks. For the Sokoban walk, which pushes a single obstacle, we recover the known critical density $\rho_c = 1-(z-1)^{-2}$ and the behavior of the escape probability close to $\rho_c$, and derive its small-density asymptotics. For the second-order pushy walk, which can push trains of two obstacles, we establish the conjectured $\rho_c = 1-(z-1)^{-3}$, and compute the escape probability at all densities. Beyond escape, the same framework yields the statistics of the trace of trapped walkers: the distribution of the maximal depth follows from the extinction probabilities, while the distributions of the visited volume and of the number of dead ends are encoded in explicit functional equations for their generating functions, which we invert numerically. All predictions agree with Monte Carlo simulations.

### Paper [272]
Title: Pseudo-entropy in Thouless Pumps and Majorana Topological Phases
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.27846
Authors: Nabaraj Khatri, Shuvam Chudal, Ashish Khanal
Abstract: We study the average excess pseudo entropy, $\Delta S_{12}$, in the Rice-Mele model under Thouless pumping. We compute it for two driving schemes, the control-freak and smooth pumps, under open and periodic boundary conditions. We find that $\Delta S_{12}$ distinguishes the trivial, monomer, and topological stages of the pump, and that it depends on the boundary condition, the driving scheme, and the reference time. We also find that a nonzero staggered potential breaks chiral symmetry and gaps out the SSH critical point, thereby flattening the pseudo-entropy peak at the phase boundary. We also study reference-state dependence in the open Kitaev chain, comparing a fixed even-parity target with five reference families: even free-fermion, pairing-only, vacuum, and two fixed gapped states. We find that the pseudo-entropy response depends on the reference and on the parity of the subsystem size, even when the reference remains gapped. At $\mu=0$, the pairing-only reference gives the exact result $S_{\mathrm{pr}}=\ln 2$ for $L=4m+2$, while the $L=4m$ sequence has analytically determined finite-size corrections. These results show that, in both models, pseudo entropy is a property of the reference-target pair and the subsystem partition, rather than a reference-independent signature of the topological phase.

### Paper [273]
Title: Twisted light affects ultrafast demagnetization
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2206.07502
Authors: Eva Prinz, Paul Herrgen, Jonas Hoefer, Benjamin Stadtm\"uller, Martin Aeschlimann
Abstract: Irradiation with an ultrashort laser pulse can completely destroy the magnetic order of ferromagnetic thin films on the femtosecond timescale. This phenomenon holds great potential for ultrafast spintronics and information processing and is an active field of research. It is still an open question if the angular momentum of light can support this effect. While it has been shown that the spin of light only has a negligible influence, we experimentally demonstrate the influence of photonic orbital angular momentum (OAM) on the magnetization dynamics of a thin nickel film. Our results reveal that the photonic OAM affects the demagnetization behavior by a non-coherent, secondary process within the first hundreds of femtoseconds depending on the handedness of the OAM with respect to the direction of the sample magnetization.

### Paper [274]
Title: Self organisation of invasive breast cancer driven by the interplay of active and passive nematic dynamics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.01285
Authors: Pablo Gottheil, Saraswat Bhattacharyya, Philip Friedrich, Kilian Roth, Mario Merkel, Kolya Lettl, Kudor Harb, Salvador Rivera-Moreno, Assal Daneshgar, Jonas Wieland, Bahriye Aktas, Jens-Uwe Blohmer, Hans Kubitschke, Anne-Sophie Wegscheider, Christine Sers, Igor M. Sauer, Armin Jarosch, Axel Niendorf, Julia M. Yeomans, Josef A. K\"as
Abstract: In invasive breast cancer, cell clusters of varying sizes and shapes are embedded in the fibrous extracellular matrix (ECM). Although the prevailing view attributes this structure to increasing disorder resulting from loss of function and dedifferentiation, our findings reveal that it arises through a process of active self-organization driven by cancer cell motility. Simulations and histological analyses of tumours from over 2,000 breast cancer patients reveal that motile, aligned cancer cells within clusters move as active nematic aggregates through the surrounding highly aligned ECM fibres, which form a confining, passive nematic phase. Cellular motion leads to cluster splitting and coalescence. The degree of cluster activity, combined with heterogeneity in cell motility, is reflected in specific scaling behaviours for cluster shape, size distribution, and the distance between cluster boundaries and nematic defects in ECM alignment. Increased activity estimates correlate with tumour progression and are associated with a poorer prognosis for patients.

### Paper [275]
Title: Glued tree lattices with only compact localized states
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22843
Authors: Andrew Osborne, Ciro Salcedo, Andrew A. Houck
Abstract: Flat band physics is a central theme in modern condensed matter physics. By constructing a tight--binding single particle system that has vanishing momentum dispersion in one or more bands, and subsequently including more particles and interactions, it is possible to study physics in strongly interacting regimes. Inspired by the glued trees that first arose in one of the few known examples of quantum supremacy, we define and analyze two infinite families of tight binding single particle Bose--Hubbard models that have only flat bands, and only compact localized states despite having any nonnegative number of translation symmetries. The first class of model that we introduce is constructed by replacing a sufficiently large fraction of the edges in a generic countable graph with glued trees modified to have complex hoppings. The second class arises from thinking of complex weighted glued trees as rhombi that can then be used to tile two dimensional space, giving rise to the familiar dice lattice and infinitely many generalizations thereof, of which some are Euclidian while others are hyperbolic.

### Paper [276]
Title: Nonequilibrium protein complexes as molecular automata
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15603
Authors: Jan Kocka, Kabir Husain, Jaime Agudo-Canalejo
Abstract: Biology stores information and computes at the molecular scale, yet the ways in which it does so are often distinct from human-engineered computers. Mapping biological computation onto architectures familiar to computer science remains an outstanding challenge. Here, inspired by Crick's proposal for molecular memory, we analyse a thermodynamically-consistent model of a protein complex subject to driven, nonequilibrium enzymatic reactions. In the strongly driven limit, we find that the system maps onto a stochastic, asynchronous variant of cellular automata, where each rule corresponds to a different set of enzymes being present. We find a broad class of phenomena in these 'molecular automata' that can be exploited for molecular computation, including error-tolerant memory via multistable attractors, and long transients that can be used as molecular stopwatches. By systematically enumerating all possible dynamical rules, we identify those that allow molecular automata to implement simple computational architectures such as finite-state machines. Overall, our results provide a framework for engineering synthetic molecular automata, and offer a route to building protein-based computation in living cells.

### Paper [277]
Title: Effective Dynamics for Weakly Interacting Bosons in an Iterated High-Density Thermodynamic Limit
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.00839
Authors: Daniele Ferretti, Kalle Koskinen
Abstract: We study the time evolution of weakly interacting Bose gases on a three-dimensional torus of arbitrary volume. The coupling constant is supposed to be inversely proportional to the density, which is considered to be large and independent of the particle number. We take into account a class of initial states exhibiting quasi-complete Bose-Einstein condensation. For each fixed time in a finite interval, we prove the convergence of the one-particle reduced density matrix towards the projection onto the normalised order parameter describing the condensate - evolving according to the Hartree equation - in the iterated limit where the volume (and therefore the particle number), and subsequently the density go to infinity. The rate of convergence depends only on the density and on the decay of both the expected number of particles and the energy of the initial quasi-vacuum state.

### Paper [278]
Title: Emergent self-inhibition governs the landscape of stable states in complex ecosystems
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.06697
Authors: Nitesh Kumar Patro, Washington Taylor, Akshit Goyal
Abstract: Species-rich ecosystems often exhibit multiple stable states with distinct species compositions. Yet, the factors determining the likelihood of each state's occurrence remain poorly understood. Here, we characterize and explain the landscape of stable states in the random Generalized Lotka--Volterra (GLV) model, in which multistability is widespread. We focus on the regime of the model where interspecies interactions are predominantly competitive and nearly symmetric. We find that the same pool of species with random initial abundances can result in different stable states, whose likelihoods typically differ by orders of magnitude. A state's likelihood increases sharply with its total biomass, or inverse self-inhibition. We develop a simplified model to predict and explain this behavior, by coarse-graining ecological interactions so that each stable state behaves as a unit. In this setting, we can accurately predict the entire landscape of stable states using only two macroscopic properties: the biomass of each state and species diversity. Our analyses also provide insight into the biomass--likelihood relationship: High-biomass states have low self-inhibition and thus grow faster, outcompete others, and become much more likely. These results reveal emergent self-inhibition as a fundamental organizing principle for the attractor landscape of complex ecosystems---and provide a path to predict ecosystem outcomes without knowing microscopic interactions.

### Paper [279]
Title: A Polarization Hall Effect in Hydrated DNA
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.06089
Authors: Mariusz Pietruszka
Abstract: We report magnetic-field- and temperature-dependent transverse-voltage phenomena in hydrated genomic DNA. The sample is confined beneath a glass coverslip in a quasi-two-dimensional geometry and subjected to perpendicular magnetic fields, revealing sharp thresholds, staircase-like voltage plateaus, and oscillatory structures approximately periodic in inverse magnetic field. Fixed-field cooling reveals longitudinal reorganizations near 20.6 and 12.0 $^\circ$C, while a separate experiment shows large-amplitude transverse oscillations below approximately 12 $^\circ$C. Water controls and buffer-free DNA measurements support an association between the structured response and the hydrated DNA system. We propose a polarization-based interpretation of the transverse response, termed a ``polarization Hall effect,'' and discuss the observed regimes within a phenomenological framework of nonlinear dynamics and metastable configurations. These findings identify hydrated DNA as a soft-matter system exhibiting structured electrical responses jointly regulated by magnetic field and temperature.

### Paper [280]
Title: More Bang for the Buck: Improving the Inference of Large Language Models at a Fixed Budget using Reset and Discard (ReD)
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.21522
Authors: Sagi Meir, Tommer D. Keidar, Noam Levi, Shlomi Reuveni, Barak Hirshberg
Abstract: The performance of large language models (LLMs) on verifiable tasks is usually measured by pass@k, the probability of solving a problem correctly at least once in k trials. At a fixed budget, a more suitable metric is coverage@cost, the average number of unique problems solved as a function of the total number of attempts. We connect the two metrics and show that the empirically-observed power-law behavior in pass@k leads to a sublinear growth of the coverage@cost (diminishing returns). To solve this problem, we propose Reset-and-Discard (ReD), a query method of LLMs that increases coverage@cost for a given budget, regardless of the pass@k form. Moreover, given a pass@k, we can quantitatively predict the savings in the total number of attempts using ReD. If pass@k is not available for the model, ReD can infer its power-law exponent. Experiments across coding (HumanEval), math (GSM8K), and reasoning (MMLU-Pro) benchmarks, as well as real GitHub issues solved by a coding agent (SWE-bench Lite), demonstrate that ReD substantially reduces the required attempts, tokens, and USD cost to reach a desired coverage, while also offering an efficient way to measure inference power-laws. ReD's advantage is maintained for imperfect verifiers.

### Paper [281]
Title: Gradually opening Schr\"odinger's box reveals a cascade of sharp dynamical transitions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02672
Authors: Barkay Guttel, Danielle Gov, Noam Netzer, Uri Goldblatt, Sergey Hazanov, Lalit M. Joshi, Alessandro Romito, Yuval Gefen, Parveen Kumar, Kyrylo Snizhko, Fabien Lafont, Serge Rosenblum
Abstract: Quantum mechanics predicts that unobserved systems may exist in a superposition of states, yet measurement produces definite outcomes, a tension at the heart of the quantum-to-classical boundary. How the transformation between these opposing regimes unfolds as observation strength increases has remained experimentally elusive. Here, by continuously tuning the measurement strength on a superconducting qubit, we reveal that measurement-dominated dynamics emerge not gradually but through multiple sharp transitions marked by the halting of coherent oscillations, state freezing near an attracting state, and the onset of quantum Zeno dynamics. Decoherence, rather than washing out this structure, reshapes it, reordering the transitions and decoupling signatures that coincide in idealised models. These results establish that the passage from quantum dynamics to measurement-dominated behaviour unfolds through a sequence of distinct transitions governed by the interplay between observation and environment.

### Paper [282]
Title: Fluctuation imaging of disorder in monolayer semiconductors
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.07418
Authors: Tom T. C. Sistermans, Rasmus H. Godiksen, Sara A. Elrafei, Alberto G. Curto
Abstract: Monolayer semiconductors hold great potential for nanoscale electronics, optoelectronics, and photonics. Excitons dominate their optical properties. As their electric fields extend outside the monolayer, they are sensitive to their surroundings. Thus, disorder can cause exciton instability, which is detrimental to device performance and critical for scalability and reproducibility. Here, we adapt a super-resolution fluorescence fluctuation microscopy technique to image localized exciton fluctuations in monolayer semiconductors, allowing us to identify unstable spots in an otherwise continuous monolayer with constant fluorescence. These spots correspond to interfacial disorder measured by atomic force microscopy. We examine how different material interfaces influence the fluctuations by comparing several substrates and provide additional insight into the disorder behind the fluctuations using hyperspectral imaging. We also assess the reduction of disorder upon thermal annealing, evidenced by a decrease in fluctuations. Our results show that fluorescence fluctuation imaging can detect disorder features similar to those of atomic force microscopy and hyperspectral imaging, while being faster and easier to implement. Therefore, it is a promising method for evaluating the quality of monolayer semiconductors, particularly when integrated with nanostructures and heterostructures found in nano-optoelectronic devices.

### Paper [283]
Title: Neural Field Thermal Tomography: A Differentiable Physics Framework for Non-Destructive Evaluation
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.11045
Authors: Tao Zhong, Yixun Hu, Dongzhe Zheng, Aditya Sood, Christine Allen-Blanchette
Abstract: Inverse problems for stiff parabolic partial differential equations (PDEs), such as the inverse heat conduction problem (IHCP), are severely ill-posed: the forward map rapidly damps high-frequency interior structure before it reaches the boundary. Soft-constrained physics-informed neural networks (PINNs), which embed the PDE as a residual penalty, decouple the diffusivity from the surface data in their standard two-network inverse formulation, and the evaluated variants fit boundary measurements while leaving the interior field essentially untouched. We propose Neural Field Thermal Tomography (NeFTY), a hard-constrained neural field framework for label-free three-dimensional inverse heat conduction. NeFTY represents the unknown diffusivity as a continuous coordinate-based neural network, and at every optimization step passes the candidate field through a differentiable implicit-Euler heat solver with harmonic-mean interface flux, so that the simulated temperature satisfies the discretized heat equation up to the solver tolerance. Adjoint gradients propagate the surface reconstruction error back to the network weights at solver-level memory cost, making test-time inversion tractable on a single GPU. Across synthetic 3D benchmarks, NeFTY outperforms soft-constrained PINN variants, a voxel-grid baseline, and classical L-BFGS voxel inversion through the same solver on label-free volumetric recovery, and it transfers to real thermography data, surpassing classical signal-processing baselines in both defect segmentation and depth estimation. Additional details at https://cab-lab-princeton.github.io/nefty/

### Paper [284]
Title: Anomalous Transport from Effective Field Theory
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15968
Authors: R\'emy Larue, Amaury Marchon, J\'er\'emie Quevillon, Diego Saviot
Abstract: A systematic study of chiral effects is presented using an Effective Field Theory framework. By integrating out a massive Dirac fermion at finite temperature in presence of vector and axial background fields, the currents and their anomalies are computed from the path-integral. Chiral effects previously considered separately naturally arise in a unified computation, including new mass corrections. The link between each anomalous transport effect and the anomalies is clearly established, beyond the identification of their coefficients. In particular, we can appreciate how these effects are sourced by the anomalous nature of the theory even in configurations where the anomaly itself vanishes. The consistent and covariant anomalies are both encapsulated in master formulae for the currents which result from a careful treatment of the regularisation. It is finally found that, at finite temperature, the physical currents cannot be inferred simply from the Chern-Simons terms whose divergence reproduce the chiral anomalies.

### Paper [285]
Title: Fracton Topological Holography
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03582
Authors: Yu-Tao Hu, Jie-Yu Zhang, Peng Ye
Abstract: Topological Holography (TH) is a framework of representing $(d-1)$-dimensional quantum systems with a $d$-dimensional sandwich, consisting of a topological order bulk, a gapped top boundary and a dynamical bottom boundary. Dualities and non-invertible symmetries are represented by the change of top boundary in the TH framework. Existing studies have focused on sandwiches with liquid topological bulk, in this paper we examine some fracton bulks, where we call the framework Fracton Topological Holography (FTH). In particular, the X-cube FTH induces the transverse field plaquette Ising model (TFPIM) version Kramers--Wannier duality, the duality between TFPIM and the two-dimensional $90^\circ$ quantum compass model, and the self-duality of quantum compass model. Haah's code FTH induces a duality of transverse-field generalized Ising models (TFGIMs). The two examples illustrate at least a part of type-I fracton (X-cube) and a part of type-II fracton (Haah's code) models can be used to construct FTH sandwiches. Moreover, we construct an explicit Sequential Quantum Circuit (SQC) switching the top boundary of X-cube sandwich, realizing the TFPIM version KW duality.

### Paper [286]
Title: High-Power Laser Drives Motion in Ultra-thin Photonic Crystal Lightsails
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20149
Authors: Lucas Norder, Ata Ke\c{s}kekler, Richard A. Norte
Abstract: Laser-driven lightsails require reflectors that are ultralight, highly reflective and robust under intense illumination. These demands point to the thinnest reflectors that dielectric optics allows, photonic crystals, in which a perforated membrane thinner than the wavelength of light acts as a mirror. Such mirrors reflect by confining light inside the membrane, but this confinement also amplifies absorption, making ultralow-loss, tension-bearing materials essential. Stoichiometric high-stress silicon nitride uniquely meets both requirements, yet its ~1 GPa stress makes large, compliant structures difficult to release intact, and such sails have never been pushed by high-power lasers. Here we demonstrate millimeter-wide, 200-nm-thick photonic-crystal trampolines in this material, with 99% design reflectivity at 1070 nm and a static stiffness of 0.53 N/m. Radiation pressure displaces them by more than a micrometer within 50 microseconds. In air, they withstand several times the irradiance found at the Sun's surface, delivered as a coherent beam. Light reshapes the sail more than it pushes it, since modest temperature differences buckle the membrane and collapse its stiffness, so thermal deformation outpaces radiation pressure within milliseconds. The interaction and compliance that make lightsails movable also let light deform them. These results establish a testbed for high-power nanophotonics, directed-energy systems and light-driven propulsion.

### Paper [287]
Title: The Sustainability Paradox of Biodegradable Packaging Through a Life Cycle Perspective on Chitosan Based Food Packaging
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20654
Authors: Maria Cosic, Milena Cosic, Amy Kim, Siddhartha Pahari, Grace Wu, Rayna Zhou, Trisha Daftari, Shaily Gupta, Reyna Michelle Suneel, Srinivasan Latha, Rashi Sharma, Utkarsh Chadha
Abstract: Chitosan-based packaging systems are being studied as alternatives to traditional food packaging made from petroleum sources. The research that has been carried out so far has mostly focused on analyzing their material properties, formulation strategies, or biodegradable behavior. However, as a result, there is a gap left in understanding how the upstream impacts affect the functional advantages of food packaging and the end-of-life results of the formulation process. This review evaluates what trade-offs exist as well as what factors affect the sustainability of chitosan-based packages. Beginning with chitin extraction from crustacean waste, key processing steps, including demineralization, deproteinization, and nanoparticle synthesis, are assessed in terms of chemical intensity, energy demand, and associated emissions. Manufacturing routes, including solvent-based and green synthesis approaches, are compared with those of conventional plastics to identify relative environmental burdens. The use phase is analyzed with respect to antimicrobial functionality, shelf life extension, and potential reductions in food waste. End-of-life pathways, including biodegradation and composting, are evaluated alongside uncertainties related to degradation behavior and nanoparticle fate. The review takes into account life-cycle issues and offers a scientifically based viewpoint on how to differentiate between packaging applications whose environmental benefits can be determined and those whose ecological footprint, lack of functionality, and uncertainty regarding disposal make it impossible to assess sustainability.

### Paper [288]
Title: Uniaxial poroelastic tendon model with crimped fibre recruitment reveals hysteresis
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24393
Authors: Zoe C. Godard, Sarah L. Waters, Derek E. Moulton
Abstract: Fibre recruitment plays an important role in tendon. Due to their large water content, a popular modelling approach for tendons is poroelasticity. Within this framework some tendon studies have included fibres, though none have included crimped fibre recruitment. We present a one dimensional poroelastic model in which the solid skeleton is composed of a soft neo-Hookean background matrix and crimped fibrils which do not bear load (FIB model). As the tissue is stretched, fibrils are straightened and contribute to load bearing. The fibre-reinforced tissue is compared to a tissue with a purely neo-Hookean (NH) skeleton in response to a uniaxial constant applied load (loading) and release of the load (unloading). The system dynamics are governed by a diffusion equation where the diffusion coefficient depends on stiffness. Within tendon parameter ranges, the FIB model is softer than the NH model, and so approaches steady state more slowly during loading. The presence of stiff, crimped fibrils increases bulk extension without excessively straining the fibrils, providing a natural protection mechanism for the tendon's structural components to load, in agreement with experiments. During unloading, the FIB model is much slower to relax as the tissue softens due to fibril re-crimping. This asymmetry in loading and unloading manifests as a hysteresis loop in the stress-strain curve averaged over the tendon. The hysteresis is reduced with increasing applied load. To the authors' knowledge, the finding of hysteresis within this framework is novel. The inclusion of fibrils allows for clearer biological interpretation and potential comparison to data. While the stress law employed in this study is bespoke for the application at hand by accounting for crimp and fibril recruitment, other fibril constitutive laws can readily be considered and incorporated into this framework.

### Paper [289]
Title: Stationary covariance spectra of discrete-time non-normal random recurrent dynamics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31944
Authors: Jacob A. Zavatone-Veth
Abstract: Principal component analysis is widely used to characterize structure in the dynamics of recurrent neural networks. For stationary noise-driven dynamics, the distribution of variance among the principal components is determined by the spectrum of the stationary covariance matrix. While the spectral properties of this matrix are well-understood for linear networks with normal synaptic weight matrices, our understanding of the stationary covariance spectrum for random non-normal dynamics remains incomplete. In this note, we use a free-probability approach to show almost-sure convergence of the stationary covariance spectrum of discrete-time dynamics with random non-normal Gaussian weights, and derive a closed functional equation characterizing its distribution. This characterization allows us to analyze the behavior of tail eigenvalues in the critical regime: they decay with the inverse-square of their rank. In contrast, applying the same approach to the analogous continuous-time dynamics leads to an infinite hierarchy of Schwinger-Dyson equations, rather than a closed scalar equation.

### Paper [290]
Title: Lattice Configuration Generation with a Self-Learning Diffusion Model
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12587
Authors: Akio Tomiya
Abstract: We show that a diffusion model for lattice field theory can be trained by self-learning, without configurations from a separate simulation, and used as the generator of an exact Markov chain. Training starts from exact samples at $\beta=0$, and along a ladder of couplings the model's own chain at each coupling, weighted by the action difference, trains the score at the next. A Metropolis-Hastings test on each proposal and its noising path keeps the sampled distribution exact for any network, so that network errors affect only the acceptance and autocorrelation times. In the two-dimensional compact XY model up to $\beta=1$, on the high-temperature side of the Berezinskii-Kosterlitz-Thouless transition, ladders at $L=4$ and $8$ reproduce exact and reference values of the energy density and helicity modulus at every coupling, and the generator trained from $\beta=0$ at $L=8$ has the same acceptance as one trained on an exact chain at $\beta=1$. The acceptance follows the relation known from hybrid Monte Carlo, with terms set by the number of noise levels and by the network error per site, and a network trained at $L=4$ generates up to $L=16$ when the number of levels grows with the volume.

### Paper [291]
Title: Comparative qualification of advanced plasma-facing materials for fusion pilot plants through public- and private-sector experiments in DIII-D
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23400
Authors: Florian Effenberg, Jonathan D. Coburn, Luca Cappelli, Simon D. Corah, Amoolya Grandhi, Jerome Guterl, Charlie Hirst, Mike Jackson, Dylan A. Kohler, Rob Kolasinski, Erick Martinez-Loran, Ria Meston, Lauren Nuckols, Angelica Ottaviano, Zana Popovic, Sergey Tsurkan, Tessa Van Volkenburg, Daniel Velazquez, Aaliyah Zuniga, Arunodaya Bhattacharya, Jose Boedo, Kent Christian, Giacomo Dose, Eric Hollmann, Mykola Ialovega, Florian M. Laggner, Samara Levine, Xavier Navarro, Andrew Shone, Lance L. Snead, David Sprouster, Jason Trelewicz, Jayson Barr, Ryan Hood, Charlie Lasnier, Yan-Ru Lin, Vincent Mazon, Jun Ren, Gilson Ronchi, Dmitry Rudakov, Dinh Truong, Cedric Tsui, Shawn Zamperini, Weicheng Zhong, Tyler Abrams, Anthony W. Leonard, Roberto Maurizio
Abstract: A coordinated DIII-D campaign involving four private fusion developers and 12 participating institutions comparatively assessed 44 advanced plasma-facing materials, including the first DIII-D divertor exposure of neutron-irradiated W, to support fusion pilot plant wall and divertor material qualification. Samples were exposed using the Divertor Materials Evaluation System (DiMES) under Ohmic, L-mode, and H-mode conditions with edge-localized modes, at 0.2-2.5 MW m$^{-2}$ on flush geometries and 10-15 MW m$^{-2}$ on 10$^\circ$ angled geometries. Engineered tungsten architectures retained integrity; long-fiber Wf/W showed the clearest crack-arrest behavior. W-Re and K-doped W showed near-ITER-W-like responses, while additively manufactured W-Ta showed heat-flux-sensitive mass losses of 0.64 mg for the flat sample and 2.19-2.87 mg for angled samples. After irradiation to 0.3 dpa at 550$^\circ$C, neutron-irradiated ITER-grade W retained 2.8 times more deuterium than pristine W, while TiB$2$ showed the lowest D$2$ release in the Ohmic set. VTaHfMo was the most stable refractory multi-principal-element alloy. NbC and (Nb${0.5}$Ta${0.5}$)C retained integrity with 0.02-0.03 mg mass loss, whereas ZrC lost 7 mg. CVD SiC retained macroscopic integrity but exhibited an effective Si erosion yield of 0.5, about 5-10 times above prior DIII-D trends. Renewable boron pebble rods underwent plasma-driven recession; 13% of released boron was ionized near the outer strike point and up to 50% was recovered locally. Initial in-situ Cr gross-erosion measurements yielded effective erosion yields of order $10^{-2}$. Together, these results provide cross-material benchmarks for fusion pilot plant down-selection and future AI/ML-assisted plasma-facing-material development.

### Paper [292]
Title: Micromotion and topological constraints on Floquet-state quantum geometry
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13649
Authors: Arpit Raj, Johannes Mitscherling, Bj\"orn Trauzettel
Abstract: The quantum geometry of Floquet-Bloch states in periodically driven crystals evolves throughout each drive cycle. We show that Floquet topology and micromotion constrain this evolution. Using a projector formulation that separates stroboscopic and micromotion contributions, we bound the deviation of the integrated quantum metric of any Floquet-band subspace from its stroboscopic value and derive a geometric speed limit on its evolution. In chiral Floquet systems, these bounds extend the topological lower bounds set by the $0$- and $\pi$-gap invariants at two symmetry-selected times into finite intervals of the drive, a dynamical topological obstruction. When both invariants are nonzero, it can force a minimum intra-period change in the metric, driven by micromotion. Because the integrated metric is a periodic lower bound on the total spread of time-evolved Wannier functions, such a change can make this spread breathe. An exactly solvable driven spin chain realizes this obstruction and further hosts a flat-band point where each band's metric equals the breathing of its compact localized state. This equality provides a direct geometric characterization of local micromotion, accessible through local spin correlators.

### Paper [293]
Title: Timelike Entanglement from Spacetime Density Matrices: A Lattice Realization
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10967
Authors: Hao-yu Wang, Wu-zhong Guo
Abstract: We develop a systematic framework for timelike entanglement in a two-dimensional free real scalar field, connecting spacetime density matrices with Lorentzian twist-operator correlators through independent microscopic and continuum calculations. Extending Gaussian diagonalization to generally non-Hermitian reduced operators, we determine their complete nonzero spectra in the generic regular case, together with all integer R\'enyi moments. The real-time replica construction relates these moments to twist correlators, with quantitative agreement in both magnitude and phase for the full four-point function on a circle, the Dirichlet and Neumann boundary two-point functions, and the massive form-factor predictions. In the massless continuum theory with Dirichlet boundaries, we further establish a finite causally connected window in which all integer moments are real, while the even moments are strictly positive for arbitrary initial states. For Gaussian states, all integer moments are strictly positive, and the resulting R\'enyi entropies are real. These results demonstrate that causal connectedness alone is not sufficient for a nonzero imaginary part of timelike R\'enyi entropy.

### Paper [294]
Title: Physics as the label for measuring and correcting materials reasoning in multimodal models
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12181
Authors: Hasan Kurban, Rasul Khanbayov, Mustafa Kurban
Abstract: Vision-language and language models increasingly interpret materials data, yet most benchmarks score final answers rather than whether the quantities a model states while reasoning obey established physical constraints. We introduce MatPCR, a label-free benchmark that uses six programmatic oracle families spanning diffraction geometry, microscopy scale, Raman peak structure, and Materials Project-grounded checks of near-hull stability, computed band-gap class, and net magnetization to test the physical admissibility of each checkable quantity a reasoning chain states, summarized as the Physical-Consistency Rate (PCR). The oracles are necessary-condition checks; their precision is audited by human review with interval estimates, and their recall on real responses is not assumed. Across nine models and 4,334 checkable records, overall PCR ranges from 0.556 to 0.778, and paired item-level tests separate only differences larger than about 0.07. Constraint-Grounded Self-Verification reduces oracle-detected violations in all four tested models; paired before-after scoring shows that it also raises answer correctness, but that most revisions which clear the oracle move to an admissible rather than an exact value. An open verifier reaches ROC AUC = 0.850 in distribution, varies strongly across constraint families, and does not transfer at a useful level to any held-out constraint family. An exact identity quantifies how oracle error displaces the reported PCR.

### Paper [295]
Title: Pauli Spectra as a Boundary Spectroscope in Quantum Spin Chains
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27537
Authors: M. A. Rajabpour
Abstract: We show that Pauli spectra provide a boundary spectroscope in critical quantum spin chains, resolving microscopic boundary fingerprints and finite responses to physical boundary changes. Universal logarithmic corrections to stabilizer Renyi entropy can remain identical across distinct boundary configurations, while their finite contributions retain information about how the physical boundaries are related. Using critical Ising chains, we demonstrate that suitable combinations of Pauli moments isolate normalization-independent responses to boundary reversal and reveal nonadditive contributions associated with the joint boundary configuration. These responses become analytically accessible through an exact term-by-term correspondence that organizes individual Pauli magnitudes into classical-group statistical structures, with their moments represented by discrete Selberg sums associated with unitary, orthogonal, and symplectic ensembles. In particular, fixed-boundary reversal admits an exact symplectic-character representation, while the same framework provides access to boundary-sensitive behavior at the R\'enyi transition. Our results establish a new connection between nonstabilizerness and boundary criticality through the exact statistical structures of classical-group ensembles.

### Paper [296]
Title: Scaling Density Functional Theory with Gaussian Splatting
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31483
Authors: Andr\'es Guzm\'an-Cordero, Cindy Zhang, Majdi Hassan, Marta Skreta, Kirill Neklyudov, Matija Medvidovi\'c
Abstract: Density functional theory (DFT) strikes a practical balance between accuracy and computational cost in many problems of computational chemistry and materials science. However, many DFT calculations are limited by fixed atom-centered basis sets, which dictate how accuracy and cost scale with system size. We propose Gaussian Splatting for Density Functional Theory (GS-DFT), which represents molecular orbitals as a cloud of Gaussians whose positions, shapes, and mixing coefficients are optimized jointly by gradient descent to minimize the energy without training data. Conceptually, GS-DFT is 3D Gaussian splatting with the renderer replaced by quantum mechanics. We introduce two key solver components: adaptive density fitting with screening for efficient evaluation of two-electron integrals, and a regularized differentiable orthogonalization of the molecular orbitals. Empirically, the optimized basis reaches the accuracy of the largest conventional basis sets with a fraction of the parameters, converging systematically in energy, density, and nuclear forces. At equal parameter count, it captures the stretched-bond and anion physics that fixed bases only recover with specialized basis augmentation. The resulting solver exhibits quadratic peak memory scaling in the cloud size, allowing us to simulate systems of up to 2,742 atoms (10,406 electrons) without any modifications at triple-zeta scale using a single four-GPU node.

### Paper [297]
Title: CBTV: Colour Bean Tracking Velocimetry for Dense Granular Flows
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31883
Authors: Kaveeshan Thurairajah, Zhao Pan
Abstract: In a dense granular flow, a particle that moves more than about half the spacing between frames is easily confused with a neighbour, and so dense flows are recorded at high frame rates. We present Colour Bean Tracking Velocimetry (CBTV) for particles of a few distinct colours. CBTV first matches small groups of neighbours by position and colour. It then links the remaining particles by relaxation particle tracking velocimetry (PTV) among particles of the same colour, with a search radius set in each frame from the detections. CBTV was compared with ten published trackers on 15 simulated granular flows, with every tracker given the same detections and one global setting. At 2.8 to 13 diameters per frame, on four flows, CBTV keeps 0.595 of the links, against 0.338 for the best published tracker on each cell. It leads that tracker on every cell outside a rotating drum, where every method fails, and trails on none. CBTV was configured on these four flows with their ground truth in view. At steps 1 to 8, on 45 benchmark cases not used in its tuning, CBTV leads the best published tracker on 14 cases and ties on 31, with no deficit. This test is not fully out of sample, because one setting was fixed after a look at part of the benchmark. The ties lie at small displacements. On two unseen flows, a colour-gated relaxation variant built in this work kept more links than CBTV on average. All tests are simulated.

### Paper [298]
Title: Stagewise and interpretable emergence of inside-outside computation in Transformer-based language models
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14221
Authors: Jack T. Parley
Abstract: Variability in syntactic structure and parsing ambiguity are fundamental aspects of natural language which modern LLMs have to learn to deal with proficiently. Previous "post-mortem" studies have shown that fully trained Transformers indeed approximate canonical parsing algorithms such as inside-outside, but little is established theoretically about how this is actually learned. Here, we make a first step towards addressing this by studying the learning dynamics in synthetic context-free grammars with variable parse tree topology. The stagewise learning curves are fully interpretable, in the sense that all of the loss stages a) can be computed analytically, and b) can be clearly associated to the representation of a hidden latent tree variable, which allows to lift ambiguity and utilize information from wider contexts. By probing representations, we visualize the emergence of inside-outside computation across depth and during training: while during early stages local (bigram/trigram) information is used and involves only a few initial layers, gradually the Transformer develops an encoder-decoder structure where the most abstract latent information is encoded in the middle, and the latter half of the network is used to progressively refine the final token prediction. This organization of computation is reminiscent of findings in real-world LLMs, and importantly applies here to a synthetic model which allows full analytical control of the data statistics, thus opening the door to theoretical progress.

### Paper [299]
Title: The bias interval of the elephant random walk
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14576
Authors: Arjun Pemmasani
Abstract: Gu\'erin, Laulin, Raschel, and Simon conjectured that the initial biases giving the superdiffusive elephant random walk a bimodal limit law form an interval centered at one half, and they described the problem as challenging. We prove this. For every superdiffusive memory the limit density has two local maxima exactly when the bias lies in a proper open interval, and outside it, including its endpoints, the density has a unique local maximum. The interval is nonempty exactly above a single memory threshold, numerically about $0.6688$. Near this threshold $a_*$ the distance between the two modes is of order $(a-a_*)^{1/2}$, and the width of the interval is of order $(a-a_*)^{3/2}$. The bias boundary is continuous and tends to zero at least quadratically fast as memory tends to one. The proof bounds the sign changes of the first and second differences of the finite walk, including those created at the boundary, and passes them to the limit.

### Paper [300]
Title: Lie-Poisson Formulation of Dissipative Spin Hydrodynamics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22615
Authors: Zi-Hao Liu, Mei Huang
Abstract: Covariance and the second law cannot fix how fluid flow couples to spin, since reversible couplings produce no entropy. We show that the spin commutation relations and the Poincar\'e algebra fix these couplings. Reversibly, a flow exerts no torque on the spin: its magnitude per particle is conserved, its direction is Fermi--Walker transported, and spin--shear couplings reflect a deforming triad. The magnitude changes only through higher multipoles, as in spinor condensates, or dissipation, whose first-order spin--shear coupling reorients the spin away from the vorticity above a critical shear rate.

### Paper [301]
Title: Construction of a Deployable Closure Model for the Transport of Turbulence Dissipation Rate with Hard-Constrained Physics-Informed Machine Learning
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23539
Authors: Tamerlan Hajiyev, Emre Sefer, Ivan Oti\'c, Ismail Ari, \"Ozg\"ur Ertun\c{c}
Abstract: We present a hard-constrained physics-informed machine learning (PIML) method for constructing a deployable closure model for the transport of turbulence dissipation rate. The framework is built from modules, each performing a single task. The closure module represents the closure with a small network, while the state module represents the flow state during training. The closure network is coupled to the state network through the governing equation residuals. This coupling allows us to learn the closure without using reference closure values as training targets. The coordinate module learns monotone input coordinates that help both networks resolve sharp changes. Within the network architecture, the boundary condition (BC) module imposes boundary values exactly using the Theory of Functional Connections, and the realizability module keeps the closure realizable. We study decaying homogeneous isotropic turbulence (HIT) and learn $\psi$, the closure function governing dissipation rate decay, from trajectories obtained in experiments and direct numerical simulations. Here, the state module is a Deep Operator Network (DeepONet), the BC module imposes the initial state and the closure's low and high Reynolds number limits, and the realizability module ensures growth of the Taylor microscale. The framework reduces the root mean square of the closure variation across ten random seeds by about 95\% relative to the unconstrained model over the training and held-out range. The deployed 18-parameter closure network requires only the Taylor scale Reynolds number $Re_{\lambda_g}$ and can be exported as a standalone C function.

### Paper [302]
Title: Efficient Spectra and Dynamics with Tangent-Space Chebyshev Tree Tensor Networks
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23975
Authors: Zhangyu Yang, Xingyao Wu, Shuo Sun, Yu Wang, Weitang Li, Jan von Delft, Christian B. Mendl, Zhigang Shuai
Abstract: We present a tangent-space Chebyshev method (TaChe), which combines tree tensor network states with Chebyshev recursion and maximum entropy spectral analysis in a tree tensor network tangent space, for quantum many-body spectra and dynamics. Implemented tensor network state calculations in the tangent space of the ground state, the core advantage of TaChe is that it can substantially reduce the computational complexity, memory usage, and numerical instability associated with tensor network state compression. Implemented on the GPU platform, it produces accurate spectra with speedups of up to $46.8$ over the previous Chebyshev tensor network method and $13.7$ over one-site time-dependent variational principle propagation in the reported benchmarks. Applications to acetonitrile, thermal aggregate emission, finite-temperature charge transport on a \(6\times6\) lattice, and internal conversion in aggregates of up to 100 molecules demonstrate the efficiency and versatility of TaChe in simulating complex molecular systems.

### Paper [303]
Title: High-Accuracy Neural-Network Quantum States via Randomized Real-Time Dynamics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26587
Authors: John M. Martyn, Rod Rofougaran, Samuel Stein, Di Luo
Abstract: Neural-network quantum states (NQSs) exhibit a disparity in converging to the ground state during energy minimization: while the energy converges quadratically with the error in the variational wave function, other observables converge only linearly, which limits the accuracy of physical predictions. Here we show how to close this gap by introducing a new algorithm that improves the accuracy of NQSs. Our method, termed dynamical averaging, estimates expectation values by averaging over a state (or observable) evolved in real-time. Using insights from quantum information, we prove an up-to-quadratic suppression of error in arbitrary ground state observables, thus matching the fast convergence of the energy. Importantly, this improved accuracy requires neither further energy minimization nor increased model complexity, but rather exploits the effect of randomization on quantum states. We demonstrate dynamical averaging on non-integrable spin models, where it accelerates convergence and substantially reduces errors in observables.
